Vienna → Charles IX and Catherine de’ Medici · homophonic cipher with word codes · 1567–69 · contributed, partial

Scipion de Fiesque at the Emperor’s court, 1567–69

A French envoy’s ciphered reports from Vienna on the King’s marriage and the German levies, deciphered by a contributor with Tomokiyo’s key from an AI transcription; checked here and built into French sentences letter by letter, with decipherments of the time found for four letters; twenty-two of the twenty-three letters give sentences, with holes, and one stays decoded, not read.

Daniel Bourdeau · posted 21 October 2026 · updated 23 October 2026

Method: read with known key · Extent: partial (81% of the cipher)

Contributed by Harumi-Matsuba (transcription, solution) and Satoshi Tomokiyo (key or decipherment used) · Also credited · Contemporary key or decipherment (key or decipherment used)

known key
On this pageThe lettersThe keyWhat was contributedMeasured hereA check against 1567The readingWhat remains uncertainFull textsSources

At a glanceKnown key applied · partial (81% of the cipher)

The letters
Scipion de Fiesque (Scipione Fiesco), French envoy to Emperor Maximilian II, to Catherine de’ Medici and Charles IX, October 1567 to November 1569: 23 letters with cipher passages, 44 pages, 29,707 signs.
Where
Bibliothèque nationale de France, Français 15919, via Gallica (btv1b90573568). No DECODE record.
The cipher
A homophonic alphabet of letters, digits and invented signs, two to five per letter, with doubled-letter signs, about a dozen word codes and null groups.
How it was deciphered
Satoshi Tomokiyo reconstructed the key for this volume; Harumi-Matsuba transcribed every cipher page with AI models and decoded it. Here the key was re-applied, the decodes scored and checked against the decipherment of the time on the facing page, and every letter built into French sentences from the decode (22–23 October 2026), with the doubtful places re-read on native-resolution images. Four letters follow decipherments of the time found in the volume.
What it says
Levies in Germany for the Huguenots, the death of Don Carlos and the marriage negotiation, Archduke Charles’s journey to Spain, and after Moncontour the Emperor’s offer to mediate.
Still open
The letter of 10 September 1569, whose reading leans on too many look-alike sign choices; phrase holes in five letters and about 90 single-word holes; a few lone codes. The transcription is AI-only.
Credit
Reading contributed by Harumi-Matsuba (issue #52), made entirely with AI models (Claude; OpenAI Codex and Claude Sonnet for the cross-checks). No human has checked the transcription. Their figures, 85.0–93.6% agreement between two AI transcriptions and a shuffled-key control, measure consistency, not sense. Key by Satoshi Tomokiyo.

01 The letters

Scipion de Fiesque, a Genoese in French service, was Charles IX’s envoy at the court of Maximilian II from the autumn of 1567, when the King’s marriage to the Emperor’s daughter Elisabeth was being negotiated. BnF Français 15919 (formerly Harlay 238) holds 174 folios of his letters to the King and to the Queen Mother. Most open and close in clear and carry the sensitive part in cipher. Satoshi Tomokiyo noted that many are partly in cipher and reconstructed the key so that the passages with no decipherment of the time could be deciphered.

Harumi-Matsuba’s page list (44 pages) runs from October 1567 to 4 November 1569: eighteen letters to the Queen Mother and five to the King (15 February 1568, 4 September 1568, 10 September, 20 October and 29 October 1569). One is dated from Speyer (2 December 1568), the rest from Vienna. The per-page table, with view numbers, sign counts and a one-line summary, is in contributed/docs/PAGES.en.md in the repository folder.

02 The key

Tomokiyo’s table (Cryptiana, “French Ciphers for German (and Eastern) Affairs”) gives each letter two to five signs: digits (4 = b, 1 and 8 = i, 9 = n), letters used as signs (a and o = e, x = f, p = l, u = o), and invented forms (Δ = i, ϕ = m, ⊢ = q, ⊣ = r, ß = r). There are signs for doubled letters (ee, ff, ll, mm, nn, rr, ss), word codes (que, qu’il, et, vous, pour, le Roy, l’Empereur, and Φ for Saxe) and groups of clear-looking letters (par, moy, bro) that are nulls. The contributor named the signs (key.tsv, 95 entries) and inferred 23 values the table lacks, among them 16 = ce, ρ = e and the pair ꝑꝑ = ff. All 44 pages are in this one key.

03 What was contributed

Harumi-Matsuba opened issue #52 on 20 October 2026 and extended it twice the same day, with the work in a public repository (data CC BY 4.0, code MIT). The account was created on 19 October. Their own description of the method:

  • First transcription by Anthropic’s Claude, from line crops of the BnF’s full-size images.
  • Disagreements with OpenAI Codex transcriptions shown on enlarged crops to Codex and Claude Sonnet; a sign was corrected only where both agreed.
  • Afterwards, fresh Codex transcriptions made without sight of the first: 85.0–93.6% agreement by value per page (the lower run where there are two).
  • For every page, 1,000 keys with the letter values shuffled: none scores as French-like as the true key.
  • Line-by-line readings of five letters, most lines marked as inferred (word division, supplied spelling, the choice between s and p for one looped sign); mechanical decodes of all 44 pages.

A person chose the target and decided to publish; no person checked the transcription. Agreement between two AI readers measures their consistency, and a shuffled-key control shows the key is right, but neither shows how much of the text reads as sense. This project’s bar for a complete reading is 95% of the cipher read as sense, measured. So the result is recorded here as a contributed result with a partial extent.

04 Measured here

The contributed key was re-applied to every transcription with this project’s own decoder (measure.py):

  • 29,627 of 29,707 signs take a value (99.7%). That is the share of signs with a value, not of text read: see the note after this list.
  • Under the project’s period French model (fr-1530-despatches) the decodes score −2.37 to −3.42 per character; the best of 200 shuffled keys per page scores −4.38 to −5.31, and none of the 8,800 reaches the true key. Clean French scores −1.64 on the same model.
  • Split into words of a period French lexicon, 81.0% of the decoded letters fall in words, against 27.6% for shuffled keys and 90.3% for clean modern French with the spaces removed. Pages range from 68% (view 8 left) to 90% (view 155 right).

The key is right and the decodes are mostly French, but between a fifth and a tenth of the text is not yet words, which matches the contributor’s own marking of most lines as inferred.

Reading audit, 22 October 2026. Read as sentences (George Lasry’s test: can the text be built into sentences and translated?), only two pages hold: f. 79r and f. 94r in the contributed readings, on Archduke Charles’s journey to Spain, with scattered holes. The readings of ff. 39r–v, 100r and 158r have holes in most lines, and the 44 mechanical decodes are unspaced with wrong letters (0–17% of sentences clean). So that day 21 letters, and the other pages of the two, were decoded, not read: about 6% of the signs read, against the 99.7% that take a value. The re-reading that followed is in section 06.

05 A check against 1567

The volume holds one decipherment made at the time: view 23 right, headed “deschiffré de la precedente”, is a clear copy of the letter of 15 November 1567. The contributor found it but did not use it, because AI readers of its cursive agreed only 51–55% with their decode. It was transcribed here by eye from a 2,600-pixel image, as far as the end of the cipher on view 22 right (calib_v023r.txt).

Of its 1,193 letters, the contributed decode of view 22 right matches 1,000 (83.8%), and 98 of its 182 words of three or more letters come out whole (calib.py). The sense runs the same throughout: Lignerolles has left for the Duke of Saxony, it is rumoured that horse and foot are being raised in Germany for those who have taken arms against the King, the Emperor expects his mandates to stop it, he has written “de bonne encre noire” to two princes. Two places differ in content. Where the copy has “au pays du duc de Wirtemberg et du conte palatin”, the decode has the Φ code for Saxe and no palatin. And the looped sign that serves for both s and p gives desuis, sarteiment for depuis, partement. This reading of the cursive is itself not checked by a second reader.

The same check found a gap: the cipher of this letter continues on view 23 left (f. 18v), which is not in the contributed transcription. Its text is on the decipherment page, from “de son pays d’Austriche” to the signature. View 23 left was transcribed and decoded here on 23 October 2026 (13 lines, 446 signs) and checked against the decipherment, which abridges it.

06 The reading

On 22 October 2026 every letter was built here into French sentences from the key decode and translated (read/<date>_reading.md and _en.md); four letters (15 November 1567, 4 June, 21 August and 6 November 1568) follow decipherments of the time found in the volume, on view 23 right and in the margins of views 65, 103 and 125 right, three of them not recorded before. On 23 October view 6 right (the first cipher page of 11 October 1567) and view 23 left were transcribed from native-resolution crops, the sign Dz was settled as con, and the single-word holes of seventeen letters were worked through on the images (161 bracketed holes down to 90): this hand writes the number codes with z for 2 and i for 1 (“zo” = 20 pour, “i4” = 14 estre), which closes many of them. Twenty-two letters now build sentences. The letter of 10 September 1569 stays decoded, not read: its reading clears the sentence test, but 4.1% of its letters rest on look-alike sign choices that nothing outside confirms. Square brackets mark supplied letters or open codes, [?word] a doubtful word, [...] an unread place.

6 March 1568, to the Queen Mother (f. 39r): j'ay entendu que le duc Jehan Guillaume [de] Saxe, qui est allé en France pour vostre service, a amené avec luy six prescheurs qui sont d'une secte qui s'appelle Flacciani.

4 September 1568, to the Queen Mother (f. 100r; docketed “au Roy”, but the cipher addresses “Madame”), on the marriage: Il m'a dict qu'a ceste heure il n'y a plus aucune difficulté que le mariaige [ne se] face, car le prince d'Espaigne estoict mort; mais que [pp] desiroict d'estre mediateur [de] ce mariaige. The lone code pp is the King of Spain by sense.

29 October 1569, to the King (f. 158r), after Moncontour: Lequel m'a dict qu'il ne s'est meu a mettre en avant ce party que pour une sincere affection et grand desir qu'il a de [vous] veoir delivré de ceste guerre intestine.

The mechanical decodes of all 44 pages are in contributed/data/decoded_*.txt, the contributor’s line readings of five letters in contributed/docs/READING.en.md.

07 What remains uncertain

  • 10 September 1569: decoded, not read, for its look-alike sign choices (the y-shaped s/p signs and others); the partial reading is in read/1569-09-10_reading.md.
  • Phrase holes after the image checks, each re-read on native crops: f. 79v lines 1–3 and f. 80 lines 1 and 8 (17 July 1568); f. 100r line 18 and three words in lines 24–26 (4 September 1568); view 38 left line 17 (15 February 1568); f. 39r lines 8, 22–23 and 29 (6 March 1568). No decipherment of the time covers them.
  • Under the 95% bar. Measured on the words of the readings, six letters that build coherent sentences stay under it: 6 March 1568 (93.3%), 12 June 1568 (94.8%), 17 July 1568 (94.4%), 4 September 1568 to the King (94.0%) and to the Queen Mother (94.3%), and 20 October 1569 (94.4%).
  • About 90 single-word holes in the seventeen letters checked on 23 October, each with its figures in the reading logs.
  • View 6 right (11 October 1567): two words in lines 5–6 and about ten doubtful words; view 23 left (15 November 1567): the bearer’s name (nine signs) and about seventeen signs that the decipherment of the time abridges.
  • Lone signs and codes whose values are not settled: ϖ (the Emperor or the King, eleven places), ∞ alone, =ii, pp (the King of Spain by sense), the group ffx-3y and a few more; 16 is ce or vostre.
  • Transcription: the contributed transcription is AI-only and no person has checked it; the pages transcribed here are single AI transcriptions checked against the key. Untranscribed cipher was found on views 7 left, 110 right, 34 and 49.

Full texts

The transcription and the decryption of every document on this page, so that the reading can be used and checked. Short files are shown here; all are in the repository.

11 Oct 1567 (docket "11 octob. 1567"), to the Queen Mother (v006r, transcribed here 23 Oct 2026; v007l, v007r, v008l, v008r)

Scipion de Fiesque, French envoy at the court of Emperor Maximilian II, to the Queen Mother, Vienna, Oct 1567 (date partly conjectural), during the negotiation of Charles IX's marriage with Elisabeth of Austria. Contents (conjectural, from the contributor's page list): The Emperor says he will do his utmost for peace in Christendom; The King's ambassador to Constantinople; The ambassador sent to Constantinople; marriages of the Emperor's daughters; Portugal; He will inquire, discreetly.

Transcription

transcription_v006r.txt

# BnF fr. 15919, view 6 right (f. 2r), letter of 11 Oct 1567 to the Queen Mother: first cipher page (23 lines), not in the
# contributed transcription. Transcribed 23 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (reread round 2) from openapi.bnf.fr IIIF crops at
# native resolution (x 4700-7600, one strip per line, upscaled 1.3-2.5x for viewing). Sign names as in
# contributed/data/key.tsv. NOT checked by a human; a single AI transcription, made while reading (labels of the
# confusable shapes were chosen with the sense in view, as the contributor did for the y-shape).
# Notation: name!x = the sign is read as x, not as its key value (a look-alike alternate, counted);
#           name? = shape uncertain; ? = a sign seen but not identified / not explained by the reading;
#           struck signs are left out. Dz is the contributor's unvalued ꝺʒ sign; it reads "con" in four words here.
# The opening four lines are clear text (in the reading file).
c01: 8 a! p fu 3y D 1 fx vbar v =22 |- ptil // f vbar tri a lf -| 1 3n v Dz m a b o
c02: =18 =20 wt a! f q9 a 3n 1 -| 3p // -| b a n // =18 vd o n + wt vd // 3n D // vbar a T b o f vbar a 3n ?
c03: b -| o T 1 -|!l =14 H# tri fx vbar fu -| // 3y n o =22 p fu 3y =18 // ptil + 1 fx vbar b tri n fs =22
c04: v o 3p p // 1 m 3n // f vbar T // vd // z o v vbar a b o T D a v + -| b -| o v |- q9 1 T + 3n vbar
c05: // p // n 1!r o T vbar 1 a 3n fs o // n // fu v o D a beta a =ll 1 m tri + 3n =22 ? ? ?
c06: ? ? H# 3p + 1 fx vbar 4 1 a! 3n =23 =14 ++ tri fx vbar f a n o H#!ss // 1 -| a! |- fu o p o v 3p -| tri 3n fx o T
c07: fx 1!r a T vbar 1 a! 3n v 3y vd a tri H#!ss o f vbar ptil 3n z 4 ++ 3n + -| b -| ? o a! vbar =22 T 1 p a! T
c08: // q9 p vbar -| o v 3y fu + ptil =ll ++ 1 o 3n fs 3p -| // 3n b -| o m // +!r D o =23 f a! v a ?!s 3p // =ll!r z
c09: 3n o -| H# 1 fx vbar D a T + f fx + v vbar o b a v vd a fs vbar -| o lf a! tri 3n o =20 fu a f 1 -|
c10: // q9 3n o c + =nn a ptil 3n 3y + f |- fu o T 1 p =14 + 1 fx vbar // 1 3n v tri =22 =18 f o =18
c11: a v 3p? ?!a fu!r m 3n o p!r a 3y!z b o fx a v vbar o 4 H# =nn o + a ptil fu -| a // 1 3n T =22 3y

c13: a 3n vbar o f D 1!r H# 1 fx vbar a! 3n fx + -| o v =22 Dz T + 3n beta ++ 3y // fu ?!l vd o T + 1 fx vbar

c15: =20 fx a =23 T a 1!r +!o 1 fx vbar 4 tri o 3n // 8 v a b o fu a +!o 1 -| vbar + fu fs o p //
c16: fx 1!r o T vbar 1 a 3n fs a a! 3n c + 3n f o 3p // 1 d6 =10 fu 3n 3y + f 8 o 3p -| 1 // 3y T //
c17: vd // z o v vbar a wt a! =13 fx o 4 1 a 3n b o vd a 3n D 1 -| o v // ptil + p ++ 3n vbar o // H#!ff 1 3n
c18: D a =18 a! f // b fu a -| vbar 1 -| 8 p wt a b 1 fx vbar =23 =14 + 1 fx vbar ptil p!r // 3y =22 3y
c19: fu + 1 // 3n vbar =21 b o D a v + -| ? b -| ? o tri ?!l 3n o 3p + fu ptil H# 1 fx vbar vd + 1 3n v =13
c20: =22 b o T a lf p // 1 3n b -| o // vd -| o vd // 3n vbar D o p // Dz b 1 vbar 1 + 3n D fu
c21: fs o wt 3p v =10 =23 f // vbar fs -| 1 4 fu + 1 fx vbar n o vd // =ll a 3y!u -| =22 // p //
c22: D o v fu 3n 3y + f b o T 3p -| 1 3n n o v fx 1!r o T vbar 1 a 3n v |- p!u ? T + f vbar lf p fu v
c23: o f n 1!l 1 3n T // p o fu -| 3p // beta vbar 1 n fu =ll 1 o -| =22 // ptil 4 1 o 3n 3p fu c p 1 fx fs

transcription_v007r.txt

# BnF fr.15919 コマ7の右ページ（f.3r、コマ7左の続きと推定）暗号部分の書き起こし（2026-10-20、目視。確定ではない）
# 画像：Gallica btv1b90573568 コマ7の右（原寸）。行の画像は src/trackline.py で作った（o_L01–o_L25）。区切りの縦線と抹消が多い。

c01: T o n fx fu -| ptil // v + 3n 4 o v + 1 3n vbar 8 C pi a b 1 fx vbar =23 // 4 1 o 3n

c03: 8 fu // a fu fs + ptil v 1 H# fu -| v b o T 1 -| P o q9 + 1 -| n -| o v vbar 1 a 3n vbar o

<!-- Reading of the cipher of Fiesque's letter to the Queen Mother, Vienna, 11 Oct 1567 (BnF fr. 15919, Gallica views 6 right - 8 right), built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (reread batch b02) from the key decode of the contributed transcription (contributed/data/decoded_v007l.txt ... decoded_v008r.txt; Tomokiyo's key), word by word, reading through the known sign confusions (y-shape s/p; t-shape c/o; fu u/l; H# o/ss; a/o both e). No contemporary decipherment of this letter was found in the volume. Words in [?word] are only partly carried by the figures; [...] = not read. The opening and closing clear text was read on the Gallica image (view 6 right) and is marked ⟨ ⟩. The first cipher page (view 6 right, 23 lines, 816 signs) is not in the contributed transcription; it was transcribed and read 23 Oct 2026 (round 2) from the Gallica image, read/r2/transcription_v006r.txt and read/r2/v006r_reread_log.md: a single AI transcription, with Dz valued 'con' from four words on the page; [Dz] where that does not fit. Spelling kept, punctuation added. -->

# Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 11 October 1567 (cipher passages)

⟨Madame, ma derniere lettre a esté du [xxv?] du moys passé, et depuis n'ay peu parler a l'Empereur que mercredy dernier, car il ne bouge de sa chambre et ne se peult soustenir sur ses pieds.⟩

[v006r] Je luy dicts que, quant je prins congé de vous pour m'en venir par deça, vous me [?commandastes] d'entendre s'il estoict vray ce que luy vous avoict dict: que, se plaignant Sa Majesté des desordres qui sont a la crestienté a cause de la relligion, que […] bien qu'il estoict necessaire que les princes crestiens y meissent ung bon ordre, et que si les aultres y voulloient prandre garde, qu'il [?ne s'espargneroict] de son costé de s[e] mettre [en] peine pour venir a une bonne unyon; que s'il estoict ainsi, que vous ne vous [?espargnerez] de ceste bonne oeuvre, ains que y [?mettriez] la main de ce costé, et [?feroy] que le Roy y entendroict, encores que [Dz] son royaulme soict en bonne paix et bien obeyssant a ses commandements, pour ce qu'il seroict bien aisé de veoir toute la crestienté en bonne paix et unyon. Je priay Sa Majesté me faire ce bien de m'en dire sa volonté, affin de vous en advertir. Il me dict qu'il estoict vray que, y voiant tant de desordres, il ne pouvoict moins faire que de se plaindre [?amerement] de la condition du temps, et qu'il n'attribuoict ce [?malleur] qu'a la desunyon des princes crestiens, qui sont plus enclins a leur particullier qu'au bien publict,

[v007l] et aveugles a leur interest, ont [?faict] cognoistre le mal si avant, que de sa part il ne s'espargnera de son pouvoir pour remettre la chrestienté en bonne paix et unyon; et loue [?fort] ce bon vouloir, duquel il a tousjours bien esté asseuré, disant que vous vous estes gouvernée si saigement que le Roy et son royaume demeuroient en bonne paix et tranquillité; et remercioit Dieu de ceste bonne inspiration qu'il vous a donnée de vouloir entendre a si bonne euvre. Et pource que [ce] faict est de grand consequence et qu'il y aura beaucoup de difficultés, il y veult penser et [con]seiller, et puis apres il m'en baillera responce.

Je luy ay dict que je m'asseure que s'il y vouloit entendre a [?bon escient], que de ce costé vous donnerez a cognoistre qu'il ne tiendra a vous de y mettre ung bon ordre et unyon, et que j'espere que ce pourroict venir a une bonne conclusion, attendu que les plus grandes forces de la chrestienté sont [?soubz] les mains de luy, du Roy et du Roy d'Espaigne; qu'il n'y a doubte que le demeurant se remettra a tout ce qu'ilz en auront [?accordé], et que Sa Majesté qu'il prouffitteroict plus que les aultres, car si par son moyen lesdicts [v007r] [estoient] secouru a son besoint.

Il me dict qu'il a bien cogneu tout cela, et que Dieu sçait [con]me il a eu tousjours desir de voir [la] chrestienté en bonne unyon, et m'a dict derechef qu'il y penseroit et bien tost me bailleroit responce. Il m'a semblé qu'il a prins goust en ce propos; toutesfois n'en sçavons riens dire jusques a tant qu'on aye eu sa responce, et pour ce j'ay retenu le courrier pour vous en envoyer la resolution, et vous ay escript ceste lettre affin que ne vous mettiez en peine de si grand longueur.

Depuis [il] m'a dict qu'il auroict occasion de se plaindre du Roy des mauvais offices que son ambassadeur y faict [a] Constantinoble contre luy, mais que pour cela il ne se plaint [?poinct], car il ne le veult croire. Je luy dicts que ce qu'il me venoict de dire de l'aultre foys que j'estois icy, l'on en faisoict courir mesme bruict, que l'ambassadeur du Roy poussoict a Constantinoble le Turc de venir au dommaige de Sa Majesté, laquelle chose estoict fort loingtaine de la verité; et que je le prioys de me faire ce bien de ne croire les mauvais, et qu'il en y a par le monde qui ne cherchent que la desunion de luy et du Roy, luy remonstrant par plusieurs raisons [v008l] et qu'il pouvoict bien cognoistre que [ϖ] et vous luy avez tousjours monstré bonne affection.

Et il me dict qu'il pourroict bien estre que cest ambassadeur eust de luy mesmes faict ces mauvais offices sans [?vostre] sceu davantaige; qu'il avoict esté adverty qu'il estoict passé [a] Venise ung ambassadeur que vous envoyez [a] Constantinoble. Je luy dicts que je ne sçavois que y envoyiez, mais que j'estois bien asseuré que vous ne traicteriez chose qui soict a son prejudice, et que si cela se faisoict, ce seroict le contraire de la bonne volunté que avez de vous employer pour ladicte unyon; et l'ay supplié croire que vous y procedez de la meilleure et sincere amitye qu'il est possible. Il vous plaira de m'advertir de ce que je luy auray a dire et respondre sur ce faict, en cas qu'il m'en parle.

[?Plus] je n'ay sceu apprendre du mariaige des filles de [ϖ], sinon que en faisant le mariaige de l'aisnée, [il] vouldroict estre esleu roy des Romains, et que [...] bagues ne aultre chose touchant ce mariaige; mais qu'il pourchassoict la seconde pour le roy de Portugal; desquelles choses [ϖ] ne se [con]t[e]nte; il a esté despesché ung courrier en Espaigne, dont n'ay riens sceu entendre. [v008r] Je tache [?fort] a m'en enquerir, mais il fault que je m'y gouverne bien discretement.

⟨...⟩

<!-- English translation of read/1567-10_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026, batch b02). Same holes; ⟨ ⟩ = clear text in the letter. -->

# Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 11 October 1567 (cipher passages)

⟨Madam, my last letter was of the [25th?] of last month, and since then I have not been able to speak to the Emperor until last Wednesday, for he does not leave his chamber and cannot stand on his feet.⟩

[v006r] I told him that when I took leave of you to come here, you [?commanded] me to find out whether what he had told you was true: that His Majesty, complaining of the disorders in Christendom on account of religion, [...] well that it was necessary for the Christian princes to put good order to it, and that if the others would take heed, he [?would not spare himself] on his side to take pains to bring about a good union; that if it were so, you [?would not spare yourself] in this good work, but [?would put] your hand to it on this side, and [?would bring it about] that the King agreed to it, although his kingdom is [Dz] in good peace and well obedient to his commands, because it would be very easy to see the whole of Christendom in good peace and union. I begged His Majesty to do me the favour of telling me his will, so that I could inform you. He told me it was true that, seeing so many disorders, he could do no less than complain [?bitterly] of the condition of the times, and that he put this [?misfortune] down only to the disunion of the Christian princes, who are more inclined to their private interest than to the public good,

[v007l] and blinded by their own interest, have [?made] the evil known so far that for his part he will spare nothing in his power to restore Christendom to good peace and union; and he praises this good will [?greatly], of which he has always been well assured, saying that you have governed yourself so wisely that the King and his kingdom remained in good peace and quiet; and he thanked God for this good inspiration He has given you, to be willing to apply yourself to so good a work. And because the matter is of great consequence and there will be many difficulties, he wishes to think about it and take counsel, and afterwards he will give me an answer.

I told him I am sure that if he would set about it [?in earnest], on this side you will show that it will not be for want of you that good order and union are put [into it], and that I hope it could come to a good conclusion, given that the greatest forces of Christendom are [?under] the hands of him, the King and the King of Spain; that there is no doubt the rest will fall in with whatever they [?agree], and that His Majesty, that it would profit him more than the others, for if by his means the said [v007r] [were] helped in their need.

He told me he had well understood all that, and that God knows how he has always wished to see Christendom in good union, and told me again that he would think about it and soon give me an answer. It seemed to me that he has taken a liking to the proposal; still we can say nothing until such time as his answer has come, and so I have kept back the courier to send you the decision, and have written you this letter so that you are not troubled by so long a delay.

Since then he has told me that he would have cause to complain of the King, of the bad offices his ambassador does against him at Constantinople, but that he does not complain of it [?at all], for he will not believe it. I told him that what he had just said to me, the other time I was here, people were spreading the same rumour, that the King's ambassador was pushing the Turk at Constantinople to come and do harm to His Majesty, which thing was very far from the truth; and that I begged him to do me the kindness of not believing the ill-disposed, and that there are people in the world who seek nothing but disunion between him and the King, showing him by several reasons [v008l] and that he could well know that [ϖ] and you have always shown him good affection.

And he told me it might well be that this ambassador had done these bad offices of his own accord without [?your] knowledge, further; that he had been informed that an ambassador whom you are sending to Constantinople had passed through Venice. I told him I did not know that you were sending one there, but that I was quite sure you would negotiate nothing to his prejudice, and that if that were done it would be contrary to the good will you have to work for the said union; and I begged him to believe that you proceed in it with the best and most sincere friendship that is possible. Please let me know what I am to say and answer him on this matter, in case he speaks to me of it.

[?Further] I have not been able to learn anything about the marriage of the daughters of [ϖ] (the Emperor), except that in making the marriage of the eldest he would wish to be elected King of the Romans, and that [...] jewels nor anything else touching this marriage; but that he was seeking the second for the King of Portugal; with which things [ϖ] is not content; a courier has been dispatched to Spain, about which I have been able to learn nothing. [v008r] I am trying [?hard] to inquire into it, but I must go about it very discreetly.

15 Nov 1567, to the Queen Mother (v022r)

Scipion de Fiesque, French envoy at the court of Emperor Maximilian II, to the Queen Mother, Vienna, 15 Nov 1567, during the negotiation of Charles IX's marriage with Elisabeth of Austria. Contents (conjectural, from the contributor's page list): News after Lignerolles left; rumour of troops levied in Germany for the Protestants. The cipher continues on view 23 left, not transcribed; calib_v023r.txt is the contemporary decipherment on view 23 right, read here by eye.

<!-- Reading of the cipher of Fiesque's letter to the Queen Mother, Vienna, 15 Nov 1567 (BnF fr. 15919, Gallica view 22 right = f. 18r, and view 23 left = f. 18v), built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (reread batch b02). View 23 left's cipher (13 lines) is not in the contributed transcription; it was transcribed and decoded 23 Oct 2026 (round 2, read/r2/transcription_v023l.txt, read/r2/v023l_reread_log.md) and the v023l part now follows the cipher, checked against the decipherment, which abridges it ("Je vo.z envoye ceste lre"). # Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 15 November 1567 (cipher passage, after the contemporary decipherment)

[v022r] Madame, j'ay puis le partement de monsieur de Lignerolles, qui fut le cinquiesme de ce moys, et lequel alla
passer devers le duc de Saxe. Il n'est survenu autre chose, sinon que le bruict couroit en ceste ville qu'il se levoit
beaucoup de gens de pied et de cheval en Allemaigne pour aller en France en faveur de ceulx qui ont prins les armes
contre le Roy. J'ay parlé a l'empereur, qui m'a dict qu'il estoit adverty que l'on assembloit des gens, mais qu'il
esperoit que quand ses mandemens seroient venuz, que personne n'oseroit desobeir, et que bien tost il en auroit
nouvelles; qu'il est bien vray que l'on luy a escript que a la desrobée de par dela quelques soldatz [vont] en France,
mais non de compaignies complettes. Et sur ce que je luy disois que au pays du duc de Wirtemberg et du conte palatin se
faisoient ces amatz, il me dict qu'il a escript a ces deux particulierement de bonne encre noire, et qu'il espere qu'ilz
ne vouldront contrevenir a leur foy ne a ce qu'ilz ont juré, et que je m'asseurasse de ce qu'il m'a dict tant de fois,
qu'il veult que par experience vous congnoissiez la bonne envye qu'il a d'entretenir bonne amitié envers le Roy, et
qu'il y chemine du bon pied; dont je l'ay remercyé, et prié croire et s'asseurer que le Roy et vous n'auriez aussi
moindre affection envers luy. Il me demande tousjours comme vont les affaires de France, mais je ne luy en sçay que
dire, et les nouvelles que nous avons par deça disent la plus partie [?le] moyen de Chantonnay. L'empereur tient
ce jourd'huy une diette

[v023l] de son pays d'Austriche, qui n'est pour autre chose que pour avoir de l'argent; et apres qu'elle sera
achevée, il s'en va a Prague. [?Et] je vous envoye ceste lettre par le [?chemin] de Flandres, [?par] [?ce] [?que] [?c'est] le plus court; et [?que] je n'ay eu
responce de celles que vous ay envoyées par Suisse. [?Que] sera la fin, apres avoir prié Dieu vous donner, Madame, en
perfaicte santé tres longue et tres heureuse vie, avec l'entier accomplissement de vos desirs. De Vienne ce quinziesme
jour de novembre mil cinq cens soixante sept. Vostre tres humble et tres obeissant serviteur et subiect, Scipion Fiesco.

## Notes

- The decipherment's "vo.z" (written like "6o.z") is read as the abbreviation for "vous": "celuy que vo.z az envoyé en
  Suysse" (that you have sent to Switzerland). The cipher of view 23 left shows the code 18 (= vous) twice in the
  corresponding lines.
- 23 Oct 2026 (round 2): view 23 left's cipher decoded. It corrects three readings of the decipherment made on 22 Oct:
  "de soy va a Prague" is "il s'en va a Prague"; "ceste [?cour]" is "ceste lettre" (the decipherment's "lre"); and
  "[?Chanton]nay [?en] responce" is "je n'ay eu responce" (the decipherment's "Je / nay eu responce"): Chantonnay is not
  named here. The cipher gives "celles que vous ay envoyées par Suisse" where the decipherment has "celuy que vo.z az
  envoyé en Suysse"; the reading follows the cipher. Still open: the bearer's name (?"le …o…imin", 9 signs) and the
  phrase between "Flandres" and "court" (about 17 signs), which neither the decode nor the cramped decipherment line
  ("q... a ce ...") settles.
- Where the decode of view 22 right differs in content from the decipherment ("duc de SAXE" for "duc de Wirtemberg et
  du conte palatin"; "lo sarteiment" for "le partement"), the reading follows the decipherment (calib.txt).
Translation

1567-11-15_en.md

<!-- English translation of read/1567-11-15_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026, batch b02). Same holes. -->

# Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 15 November 1567 (cipher passage)

[v022r] Madame, since the departure of Monsieur de Lignerolles, which was on the fifth of this month, and who went by
way of the Duke of Saxony, nothing else has happened, except that the rumour ran in this city that many foot and horse
were being raised in Germany to go to France in favour of those who have taken up arms against the King. I spoke to the
Emperor, who told me that he had been warned that men were being gathered, but that he hoped that once his mandates had
arrived no one would dare disobey, and that he would soon have news of it; that it is quite true he has been written to
that some soldiers [are going] to France by stealth from over there, but not in complete companies. And when I told him
that these levies were being made in the lands of the Duke of Württemberg and of the Count Palatine, he told me that he
has written to those two in particular in good black ink, and that he hopes they will not go against their faith nor
against what they have sworn; and that I should rest assured of what he has told me so many times, that he wants you to
know by experience the real wish he has to keep up good friendship with the King, and that he goes about it in good
faith; for which I thanked him, and begged him to believe and be assured that the King and you would have no less
affection towards him. He keeps asking me how the affairs of France are going, but I do not know what to tell him, and
the news we have over here says most of it [?through] Chantonnay. The Emperor is holding today a diet

[v023l] of his country of Austria, which is for nothing other than to get money; and after it is over he goes to
Prague. [?And] I send you this letter by the [?road] of Flanders, [?because] [?it] [?is] the shortest; and [?that] I have had no answer to those I sent
you by way of Switzerland. [?What] will be the end, after praying God to give you, Madame, in perfect health a very long
and very happy life, with the full accomplishment of your wishes. From Vienna this fifteenth day of November fifteen
hundred and sixty-seven. Your most humble and most obedient servant and subject, Scipion Fiesco.

21 Jan 1568, to the Queen Mother (v034r, v035l)

# Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 21 January 1568 (cipher passage)

[v034r] [...] [ap]prendre aultres choses, sinon qu'il est deliberé que ses enfans retournent en Allemaigne le plus tost qu'il sera possible. Il m'a demandé plusieurs fois s'il y a eu nouvelles de l'arrivée de monsieur de Lignerolles, auquel je n'ay sceu dire autre chose fors que je n'ay poinct eu de lettres depuis le cinquiesme novembre. Et a ce que je puis congnoistre, je croys qu'il desire quelque responce de ces lettres pour sçavoir [con]me vous estes demeurés contens de luy. Il me semble qu'il a bonne envye d'entretenir bonne amitié, et pour le tenir en ceste bonne opinion je [v035l] [...] de maintenir ceste amitié, et qu'il continuera les anciennes confederations qui ont esté entre l'Empire et le royaulme de France.

<!-- English translation of read/1568-01-21_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026, batch b02). Same holes. -->

# Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 21 January 1568 (cipher passage)

[v034r] [...] learn other things, except that it is decided that his children shall return to Germany as soon as possible. He has asked me several times whether there was news of the arrival of Monsieur de Lignerolles, to which I could say nothing else except that I have had no letters since the fifth of November. And as far as I can tell, I believe he wants some answer to those letters, to know how satisfied you have been with him. It seems to me that he has a real wish to keep up good friendship, and to keep him in this good opinion I [v035l] [...] to maintain this friendship, and that he will continue the ancient confederations that have been between the Empire and the kingdom of France.

15 Feb 1568, to the King (v037r, v038l, v038r)

Scipion de Fiesque, French envoy at the court of Emperor Maximilian II, to the King, Vienna, 15 Feb 1568, during the negotiation of Charles IX's marriage with Elisabeth of Austria. Contents (conjectural, from the contributor's page list): The Emperor is displeased at having no news since Lignerolles left; Resolution of the diet of Fulda: the Emperor to mediate with the Prince of Condé; The troubles in France and Flanders will ruin Germany; Alba levies troops in Germany.

# Fiesque to Charles IX, Vienna, 15 February 1568 (cipher passages)

[v037r] [Je l'ay] apperceu ung peu fasché qu'il n'avoict eu de vos nouvelles depuis le partement de monsieur de Lignerolles.

(margin decipherment beside it: "apperceu ung peu fasché qu'il n'avoit eu de vos nouvelles depuis le partement de mons.r de Lignerolles")

[v038l] Mais en devisant avec luy m'a semblé qu'il trouveroict fort bon que fussiez sorty de ces peines et affaires avec honneur, par quelque bon [?appoinctement]. Et mesmes vous aurez veu par l'expedition de [?l'homme] qu'il vous envoye dimanche dernier, que la resolution de la diette de Fulda est de sçavoir s'il sera de vostre bon plaisir que l'Empire moyenne d'appoincter monsieur le prince de Condé et ceulx de sa suitte, et les remettre [en] bonne grace. Et m'a on dict que apres qu'ilz auront [?sceu] de vostre volonté, qu'ilz sont apres de faire une mesme despesche au roy d'Espaigne pour le persuader de remettre aussi ceulx de Flandres et appaiser le pays, qui est malcontant des procedures que y faict faire le duc d'Albe, lesquelles l'on ne trouve bonnes de deçà, ains trop rigoureuses; et semble a beaucoup que [?elles] ne pourront prandre bonne fin. Et entre autres le duc Auguste de Saxe [?pourchasse] fort ce faict, lequel, oultre l'alliance et grande amitié qu'il [a] avec le prince d'Orenge, craint, comme [v038r] aussi font plusieurs aultres, que ces troubles tant de France que de Flandres ne portent a la fin une grande ruyne a l'Allemaigne. Et ledict duc d'Albe monstre avoir soupçon de quelque rebellion en Flandres, parce qu'il faict une grand despence en Allemaigne, [?car] il a desja faict lever des reistres, et oultre ce entretient secrettement plusieurs cappitaines de gens de cheval et de pied [?pour] estre prests quand il les mandera.

1568-02-15_en.md

<!-- English translation of read/1568-02-15_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026, batch b02). Same holes. -->

# Fiesque to Charles IX, Vienna, 15 February 1568 (cipher passages)

[v037r] [I found him] somewhat vexed that he had had no news of you since the departure of Monsieur de Lignerolles.

[v038l] But in talking with him it seemed to me that he would be very glad if you were out of these troubles and affairs with honour, by some good [?settlement]. And indeed you will have seen, from the dispatch of [?the man] whom he sent you last Sunday, that the resolution of the diet of Fulda is to learn whether it will be your good pleasure that the Empire should mediate to reconcile the Prince of Condé and those of his following, and restore them to [your] good grace. And I am told that once they have [?learned] your will, they intend to send the same dispatch to the King of Spain to persuade him to restore those of Flanders as well and to pacify the country, which is discontented with the proceedings the Duke of Alba is carrying out there, which are not thought good here, but too harsh; and it seems to many that [?they] will not be able to come to a good end. And among others Duke Augustus of Saxony [?presses] this matter hard; besides the alliance and great friendship he has with the Prince of Orange, he fears, as [v038r] several others also do, that these troubles, both of France and of Flanders, will in the end bring great ruin upon Germany. And the said Duke of Alba shows that he suspects some rebellion in Flanders, because he is spending heavily in Germany, for he has already had reiters raised, and besides this he secretly keeps several captains of horse and foot to be ready when he sends for them.

6 Mar 1568, to the Queen Mother (f039r, f039v) read in part

Scipion de Fiesque, French envoy at the court of Emperor Maximilian II, to the Queen Mother, Vienna, 6 Mar 1568, during the negotiation of Charles IX's marriage with Elisabeth of Austria. Contents (conjectural, from the contributor's page list): Duke Johann Wilhelm of Saxony and Flacian preachers; The Emperor means to work for an end of the French civil war.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (93% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

<!-- Reading of the cipher of Fiesque's letter to the Queen Mother, Vienna, 6 Mar 1568 (BnF fr. 15919, f. 39r-v, Gallica views 43 right, 44 left), built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (reread batch b02) from the key decode of the contributed (corrected) transcription (contributed/data/decoded_f039r.txt, decoded_f039v.txt; Tomokiyo's key), word by word, reading through the known sign confusions (y-shape s/p; t-shape c/o; fu u/l; a/o both e). # Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 6 March 1568 (cipher passages)

⟨Madame, ma derniere lettre a esté du penultiesme du passé, depuis laquelle⟩ [f039r] j'ay entendu que le duc Jehan Guillaume [de] Saxe, qui est allé en France pour vostre service, a amené avec luy six prescheurs qui sont d'une secte qui s'appelle Flacciani, et luy a dict qu'avec eulx et les armes il [?espere] chastier les papistes et huguenots; de quoy je ne faisoys pas grand compte, me semblant qu'il n'avoict assez forces pour faire ung tel exploict. Mais [...] m'a dict qu'il en fault faire [?plus] d'estime que je ne pense, car il n'est [?que] [?trop] vray qu'il ne tache que croistre et augmenter ladicte secte, de laquelle il y a long temps que son frere le duc Jehan Frederic, qu'il tient prisonnier, en estoict; et depuis les a chassés, et ledict duc Jehan Guillaume les a recueillis; qu'il est a craindre [?plus], attendu que plusieurs de ses cappitaines sont parens et amys [de] ceulx du [?duc] Casimyre et quasi d'une mesme secte, que vous n'en puissiez avoir tel service que vos affaires le requerront; non pas qu'il doubte que ledict duc Jehan Guillaume vous faille ne face chose qui soict contre son honneur.

Et plusieurs [de] ceste court trouvent fort estrange que luy ayez donné si grande charge, estant beau filz du comte [⊸] qui a esté cause, [con]me il vous semble, [de] la longueur [de] ceste guerre, et que ne vous perceviez que luy et le Casimyre [?n'ont] garde [?de] se porter [...]. A ceste cause je croys qu'il seroict le grand prouffict du Roy, [?comme] je vous ay par plusieurs fois escript, que ces gens eussent vuydé le royaume, et vos forces françoises demeurassent entieres. Et mesmes [∞], qui me tint jeudy dernier [ung] long propos sur ce faict, est d'advis qu'il seroict beaucoup meilleur que [∞] [...] ung seul a rigueur envers ceulx qui [f039v] l'ont offencé, pourveu qu'ilz ne veuillent aucunes fortes places en leurs mains, et [?condescendre] a aucunes leurs demandes affin de [?se delivrer de] ces troubles, par[ce qu'il] veoid que ces guerres durent [?trop], et qu'elles ne peuvent porter que grande ruyne et dommaige a ce royaulme. Et selon la responce qu'il aura [...] qu'il vous a envoyé, il s'employera pour vous veoir hors [de] ces guerres intestines. Et a ce que je puis [?] veoir, [?comme] je vous ay cy devant escript, il attend sçavoir sur cela [la] volunté du Roy, pour apres [...] semblable despesche au roy d'Espaigne pour le prier a voir remettre en sa bonne grace ceulx de Flandres.

[Il] a envoyé ung courrier en Espaigne, qui n'estoict pour aultre chose que pour [...] et moyenner quelque reconciliation entre luy et son filz. Il est arrivé depuis ung courrier d'Espaigne, mais jusques a ceste heure je n'en ay riens sceu apprendre. Il [?est] bien vray que [?l'on] sçait la cause de la prinse du prince, laquelle il tient secrette.

⟨Madame, voyant qu'à ceste heure je ne vous puis faire aucun service ... De Vienne ce 6e de mars 1568.⟩

# Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 6 March 1568 (cipher passages)

⟨Madam, my last letter was of the last day but one of last month, since which⟩ [f039r] I have heard that Duke Johann Wilhelm of Saxony, who has gone to France in your service, has brought with him six preachers of a sect called the Flacians, and has told them that with them and with arms he [?hopes] to chastise the papists and Huguenots; which I did not make much of, as it seemed to me he had not forces enough for such an exploit. But [...] told me one must make [?more] of it than I think, for it is only too true that he strives only to spread and increase that sect, of which his brother Duke Johann Friedrich, whom he holds prisoner, was long a member; and since then he drove them out, and the said Duke Johann Wilhelm took them in; that it is to be feared [?the more], since several of his captains are relatives and friends of those of [?Duke] Casimir and almost of the same sect, that you may not get such service from him as your affairs will require; not that he doubts the said Duke Johann Wilhelm will fail you or do anything against his honour.

And many at this court find it very strange that you have given him so great a command, being son-in-law of the Count [⊸] who has been the cause, as you think, of the length of this war, and that you do not perceive that he and Casimir [?take] [?no] care to [...]. For this reason I believe it would be greatly to the King's profit, as I have written to you several times, that these people had left the kingdom and your French forces remained whole. And indeed [∞], who held a long discourse with me on this matter last Thursday, is of the opinion that it would be much better that [∞] [...] a single one to severity towards those who [f039v] have offended him, provided they do not want any strongholds in their hands, and [?to yield] to some of their demands in order [?to be rid of] these troubles, because he sees that these wars last [?too long] and can bring only great ruin and damage to this kingdom. And according to the answer he will have [...] that he has sent you, he will exert himself to see you out of these civil wars. And as far as I can see, as I wrote to you before, he is waiting to learn the King's will on this, so as afterwards to [...] a similar dispatch to the King of Spain to beg him to be willing to restore those of Flanders to his good grace.

He has sent a courier to Spain, who was for nothing else than to [...] and to negotiate some reconciliation between him and his son. A courier from Spain has since arrived, but up to this hour I have not been able to learn anything of it. It is quite true that [?people] know the cause of the arrest of the prince, which he keeps secret.

⟨Madam, seeing that at present I can do you no service ... From Vienna this 6th of March 1568.⟩

27 Mar 1568, to the Queen Mother (v049r)

Scipion de Fiesque, French envoy at the court of Emperor Maximilian II, to the Queen Mother, Vienna, 27 Mar 1568, during the negotiation of Charles IX's marriage with Elisabeth of Austria. Contents (conjectural, from the contributor's page list): The marriage; the King of Spain; Grignan.

# Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 27 March 1568 (cipher passage)

[v049r] [...] en propos du faict du mariaige, m'a dict que [?ce] qu'il vous en avoict escript, n'estoict de tenir le faict en aucune longueur, mais qu'il falloict qu'il attendist lettres d'Espaigne, car il ne sçavoict encores en quels termes estoient les affaires du prince d'Espaigne; et que apres vous verries qu'il ne tiendroict a luy que le mariaige ne se feit bien tost. Je luy ay dict que ledict sieur de Grignan s'en alloict bien contant, voyant qu'il pouvoict asseurer le Roy et vous de la bonne volunté qu'il vous porte, [au] Roy, et que j'esperois en Dieu que bien tost le mariage se pourroict accomplir, attendu que le commencement estoict bon, et que les deux parties y procedoient de bonne affection. Et sur ce l'Emperatrice est venue souper avec luy, que a esté cause que je ne luy ay peu tenir plus long propos; et n'ay pas voulu faillir de vous escripre cecy, affin que vous sçachiez qu'il n'a moindre envye [que] vous que bien tost le mariaige se f[ace].

# Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 27 March 1568 (cipher passage)

[v049r] [...] on the subject of the marriage, he told me that [?what] he had written to you about it, it was not [his intention] to keep the matter in any delay, but that he had to wait for letters from Spain, for he did not yet know in what state the affairs of the Prince of Spain stood; and that afterwards you would see that it would not be for want of him that the marriage was not soon made. I told him that the said Sieur de Grignan was leaving well content, seeing that he could assure the King and you of the good will he bears you [and] the King, and that I hoped in God that the marriage could soon be accomplished, since the beginning was good and both parties were proceeding with good affection. And thereupon the Empress came to sup with him, which was the reason I could not hold longer discourse with him; and I did not want to fail to write you this, so that you know that he has no less desire [than] you that the marriage be made soon.

4 Jun 1568, to the Queen Mother (v065r)

Scipion de Fiesque, French envoy at the court of Emperor Maximilian II, to the Queen Mother, Vienna, 4 Jun 1568, during the negotiation of Charles IX's marriage with Elisabeth of Austria. Contents (conjectural, from the contributor's page list): No decision from Spain yet on the marriage.

# Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 4 June 1568 (cipher passage)

[v065r] Il ne s'est jamais [?presenté] occasion de toucher aulcuns propos sur le faict du mariage a l'empereur, lequel
ne m'en a aussi rien dict; et a ce que je puys entendre, il n'a eu aulcune resolution d'Espaigne, sans laquelle je croy
qu'a grand peine se resouldra il de conclure le mariage avec le Roy, pour l'effect duquel l'on dict en ceste court que
vous luy envoyez ung ambassadeur.

## Alignment notes

- Decode: "ET aoncasiondetoucheraucunsroncssurle faictdumariaigea LE ROY le QUE unemenauti ET riensdict ET adne QUE
  iesuisentandreiln euaucuneresolutiondessaignecisanshe uacue LL eienrroysquagrandseineseresouldr iude?clu[?]elemariaige
  auec LE ROY gesgele FF et ducuelucndictencesteccurt QUE uousluy enuoyesiungamcaoadece".
- The first ∞ ("a LE ROY") is l'empereur in the margin (the tailed ∞~, not distinguished in the transcription); the
  second is le Roy. "amcaoade" = ambassade(ur), H# = ss.
- The margin's second word of line 2 is cramped ("[?presenté]"); the decode begins "[…] a occasion", so the opening verb
  is not carried by the figures either.

# Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 4 June 1568 (cipher passage)

[v065r] No occasion has ever [?arisen] to raise anything with the Emperor about the marriage, and he has said nothing to
me about it either; and as far as I can tell, he has had no resolution from Spain, without which I believe he will
hardly bring himself to conclude the marriage with the King, for the effecting of which it is said at this court that
you are sending him an ambassador.

12 Jun 1568, to the Queen Mother (v070r, v071l) read in part

Scipion de Fiesque, French envoy at the court of Emperor Maximilian II, to the Queen Mother, Vienna, 12 Jun 1568, during the negotiation of Charles IX's marriage with Elisabeth of Austria. Contents (conjectural, from the contributor's page list): The Spanish prince's detention; the daughters' marriages; Nothing will be decided before the answer from Spain.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (95% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

# Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 12 June 1568 (cipher passage)

[v070r] [con]me [ϖ] m'avoict dict que le sieur [?Dietri]stan luy avoict escript par lettres du douziesme du passé que le roy d'Espaigne estoict apres a luy envoyer ung courrier, par lequel il l'adviseroict et de la cause de la retention du prince son filz, que de la resolution du demeurant, qui est touchant le faict du mariaige de ses filles. Ainsi que j'entendray que ledict courrier sera venu, je chercheray occasion de sçavoir […] de luy [con]me il a deliberé s'en resouldre. Et a ce que je puis entendre, [il a] la grande envye que sa fille aisnée soict bien tost mariée, laquelle le premier de novembre achevera dix-neuf ans, et la seconde en ce moys en a achevé quatorze.

J'ay entendu de bonne part, qui m'a dict qu'il est adverty que vous avec tout le conseil du Roy avez deliberé que [ϖ] se marye, et que estiez apres a luy envoyer monsieur le duc de Montpensier ou autre grand personnaige de ce royaulme. Et demandant a celuy ce que [ϖ] disoict sur ce, m'a dict qu'il n'en disoict aultre chose sinon que avez deliberé d'en sçavoir a ce coup la resolution. Toutesfois, continuant a mon [v071l] opinion, je croys que a grand peine il ne resouldra aucune chose qu'il n'aye eu responce d'Espaigne, laquelle j'espere ne tardera guieres a […].

# Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 12 June 1568 (cipher passage)

[v070r] As [ϖ] (the Emperor) had told me that the Sieur [?Dietrich]stein had written to him by letters of the twelfth of last month that the King of Spain was about to send him a courier, by whom he would inform him both of the cause of the detention of the prince his son and of the decision on the rest, which concerns the marriage of his daughters. As soon as I hear that the said courier has come, I will look for an occasion to learn […] from him how he has decided to settle it. And as far as I can understand, [he has] a great wish that his eldest daughter be married soon, who on the first of November will complete nineteen years, and the second this month has completed fourteen.

I have heard on good authority, from one who told me, that he has been informed that you, with the whole of the King's council, have decided that [ϖ] shall marry, and that you were about to send him Monsieur the Duke of Montpensier or some other great personage of this kingdom. And when I asked that man what [ϖ] said about it, he told me that he said nothing else about it except that you have decided to learn the decision on it this time. Still, holding to my [v071l] opinion, I believe that he will hardly decide anything until he has had an answer from Spain, which I hope will not be long […].

17 Jul 1568, to the Queen Mother (f079, f079v, f080) read in part

Scipion de Fiesque, French envoy at the court of Emperor Maximilian II, to the Queen Mother, Vienna, 17 Jul 1568, during the negotiation of Charles IX's marriage with Elisabeth of Austria. Contents (conjectural, from the contributor's page list): Archduke Charles to be sent to Spain for the "délivrance" of Don Carlos; The affair may take long; Archduke Charles may reside in Flanders; A courier leaves for Spain tonight.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (94% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

# Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 17 July 1568 (cipher passages)

[f079] […] que le sieur […] grand [?escuyer] de l'Empereur devoict aller en Espaigne, lequel [?n'est] encores party, ne
aucun courrier; et je n'ay sceu entendre a quoy tient ceste longue demeure. L'archeduc Charles est venu icy parler a
l'Empereur, et aucuns m'ont voulu dire que c'est [?pour] la grande demeure de [la] deliberation d'y envoier [?…], pour
[?…] affectueusement [?…] la delivrance du prince; et qu'il s'est tenu quelques propos que l'archeduc Charles mesmes
entreprendroit ce voyaige, y ayans l'Empereur et l'Imperatrice fort grand desir, et estans deliberés de s'employer de
tout leur pouvoir [?pour] la delivrance dudict prince. Toutesfois je ne le puis asseurer, car les maniemens sont si
secrets que jusques a ceste heure je n'en ay riens sceu descouvrir, dont je me suis [?…], et aussi que je n'en ay ausé
toucher aucunement a l'Empereur, par ce que [?…] a noz derniers propos que eusmes ensemble, que apres qu'il auroict
despesché en Espaigne, qu'il me diroict encores combien de temps il espereroict avoir responce; et que n'estant encores
personne party, il m'a semblé que si je [?vouloys essayer] d'en sçavoir quelque chose,

[f079v] qu'il eust esté sans propos, et que ne [?…] en sorte [?…], je ne faillray vous advertir de ce que j'en [?…]
diligemment de jour en [?jour]; mais je crains que cest affaire soict fort long. Le bruict court aussi icy que la venue
de l'archeduc Charles, et pour ce qu'il se [?…] entre[…], et [ꝥ] qu'il aille resider en Flandres pour [con]soler et adoulcir ce peuple, qui est si malcontant de se veoir ainsi gouverné des Espaignols; mais quant a moy je n'y veois aucune apparance.

[f080, postscript] J'ay [?…], et j'ay entendu qu'il a faict une despesche en Espaigne, et que ceste nuict partira ung
courrier, qu'est signe que ledict sieur [?…] partira si tost, ainsi que ledict courrier sera party. J'essayeray de
sçavoir icy discretement de [ϖ] [?…], et que je vous ay cy dessus escript.

## Notes

- f079 c01-c02 "quelesieubcoangrand esounerde L'EMPEREUR": the name ("bcoan") is not read; "esouner" taken as
  [?escuyer] (as the contributor did); f080 c05 repeats "ledict sieur coan".
- f079 c07-c08 "dyenuoier scurnrieroienaoectueusemontolel deliurance": "d'y envoier" then a stretch read only as
  "[?…] affectueusement"; left open.
- f079 c17 "dontiemesuioeaoeu cer" (dont je me suis a[ss]eu…?) not settled.
- f079v c06-c08 ("ET cece QUEL semanyeentrem ET [?] QUEL ai LL eresider en flandres ET ?soler ET adcul circeseun le cuest
  simal?tant"): only "qu'il aille resider en Flandres" and "est si malcontant de se veoir ainsi gouverné des Espaignols"
  are sure; the subject of "malcontant" is not read.
- f080 c07-c08 "descaucicicdetcrement demc ET": "de sçavoir icy discretement de [ϖ]"; ϖ standing alone is the code for
  l'Empereur elsewhere in the volume (fork B; 1568-08-21 margin).
- Image checks: none in this pass (the readable stretches are carried by the decode; the open ones need sign-level
  crops of f. 79r c07-c08, c17, f. 79v c01-c03, c06-c08).

# Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 17 July 1568 (cipher passages)

[f079] […] that the lord […], the Emperor's grand [?equerry], was to go to Spain; he has not yet left, nor has any
courier, and I have not been able to learn what this long delay hangs on. Archduke Charles has come here to speak to the
Emperor, and some have tried to tell me that it is [?because of] the long delay in deciding to send [?…] there, to
[?…] earnestly [?…] the release of the prince; and that there has been some talk that Archduke Charles himself would
undertake this journey, the Emperor and the Empress having a very great wish for it, and being resolved to work with all
their power for the release of the said prince. However, I cannot vouch for it, for the dealings are so secret that up
to now I have been able to discover nothing of them, of which I have [?…], and also because I have not dared raise it at
all with the Emperor, because [?…] at our last conversation together, that after he had sent his despatch to Spain he
would tell me how long he expected to wait for an answer; and since no one has yet left, it seemed to me that if I
[?tried] to learn something of it,

[f079v] it would have been out of place, and that [?…] in such a way [?…], I shall not fail to inform you of what I
[?…] diligently from day to [?day]; but I fear this affair will be very long. The rumour also runs here of Archduke Charles's coming, and because he [?…] inter[…], and [ꝥ] that he should go to reside in Flanders to console and soothe this people, which is so discontented at seeing itself governed thus by the Spaniards; but for my part I see no likelihood of it.

[f080, postscript] I have [?…], and I have heard that he has sent a despatch to Spain, and that a courier will leave
tonight, which is a sign that the said lord [?…] will leave as soon as the said courier has gone. I shall try to learn
here discreetly from [the Emperor] [?…], and what I have written to you above.

14 Aug 1568, to the Queen Mother (f094, f094v, f095)

Scipion de Fiesque, French envoy at the court of Emperor Maximilian II, to the Queen Mother, Vienna, 14 Aug 1568, during the negotiation of Charles IX's marriage with Elisabeth of Austria. Contents (conjectural, from the contributor's page list): The Emperor explains why he sends Archduke Charles to Spain; The marriage will be settled on the Archduke's return; The Duke of Bavaria's second son will go to Spain with the Archduke.

# BnF fr.15919 f.94r（「14 Aoust 1568 Vienne」「du Comte de Fiesque a la Reyne」）暗号部分の書き起こし（2026-10-20、目視。確定ではない）
# 画像：Gallica btv1b90573568 コマ99の右（原寸）。行の画像は src/trackline.py で作った（f_L02 が暗号の始まる行）。

# c01 の前は平文「Madame, Jay receu vostre lettre du 9e de Juillet par laquelle me escripvez」。続く「fi ol par moy」は無効字

1568-08-14_reading.md

<!-- Reading of the cipher of Fiesque's letter to the Queen Mother, Vienna, 14 Aug 1568 (BnF fr. 15919, Gallica views 99 right = f. 94r, 100 left = f. 94v, 100 right = f. 95r), built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (reread batch b02) from the key decode of the contributed transcription (contributed/data/decoded_f094.txt, decoded_f094v.txt, decoded_f095.txt; Tomokiyo's key), word by word, reading through the known sign confusions (y-shape s/p; cross shapes c/o/q/r; a/o both e; H# = ss in "ambassade", "asseurement"). The contributor's reading of f. 94r (READING.en.md) used as a check only. The clear text before the cipher ends "par laquelle me escripvez". No contemporary decipherment beside it. Spelling kept, punctuation added. [?word] = partly carried; […] = not read; [ϖ], [mm] = lone signs standing as codes. -->

# Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 14 August 1568 (cipher passages)

[f094] ⟨… par laquelle me escripvez⟩ que j'essaye de bien entendre par le menu ce que les courriers d'Espaigne ont
apporté, que j'ay faict le plus diligemment qu'il m'a esté possible; de quoy toutesfois je n'ay peu aprendre aultre
chose que ce que je [?vous] ay escrist par les miennes precedentes. Vous aurez sceu par celle du [?sixiesme] de ce moys
comme l'Empereur me disoict tousjours qu'il parleroict a moy. Depuis il m'a dict que ce qu'il me vouloict dire estoict
qu'il envoye son frere l'archeduc Charles en Espaigne pour deux raisons: l'une, le faict du prince d'Espaigne, et
l'autre, les affaires de Flandres, lesquelles semblent estre en estat d'avoir bon besoing de bonne provision, [?pour]
le bien du pays, repos de l'Allemaigne et de toute la chrestienté; et qu'ayant la charge qu'il a, il est contrainct d'y
faire toutes ses diligences, lesquelles il ne sçauroict faire avec plus grande efficace que d'envoyer son frere mesmes devers […] pour luy faire entendre son advis et ce que jugent estre necessaire tous les princes d'Allemaigne [?pour] le bien
de son pays de Flandres. Il sera bien aise si l'ambassade est trouvée bonne et qu'elle sorte fruict, et si elle ne
prouffite, a tout le moings sera il deschargé envers Dieu et tout

[f094v] le monde d'avoir faict son debvoir, et ce qu'appartient a ung bon frere. Il m'a aussi dict qu'au retour de
sondict frere il sera resolu touchant le faict du mariage, car asseurement il sortira a la conclusion du[dict] [?…], et
tout, ne voullant en façon du monde plus attendre; et encores qu'il ayt envoyé ung courrier devant, il n'attand aucune
responce de ce faict, par ce qu'il dict que jusques a ceste heure il a esté manié par lettres, lesquelles ne parlent
qu'une fois; mais que pour en estre mieulx satisfaict et eviter longueur et tant de parolles, il a trouvé meilleur que
son frere mesmes en sorte la conclusion, laquelle il espere avoir dans quatre moys, que le voyaige de son frere durera,
lequel partira le quatriesme de ce septembre pour s'aller, et embarquera a Genes. Et qu'a son retour il [?aimeroict] bien passer par France; dont je luy dicts que j'en serois bien aise, car je m'asseurois que [ϖ] et vous en auriez grand plaisir, et avoir occasion de le caresser et faire paroir la bonne volunté que luy avez
tousiours porté et portez encores, et a tous ceulx qui despendent de sa majesté; a ceste cause que je le priois bien
fort me faire ce bien de m'en advertir pour vous en adviser. Il m'a dict qu'il n'est encores asseuré du chemin qu'il
tiendra a son retour, mais qu'il ne faillira m'en

[f095] adviser. Je croys qu'il sera bien a propos que luy escriviez [con]me avez entendu de moy que son frere alloict en
Espaigne, et que si a son retour il vouloict passer par France, que luy offrez le pays et passaige. Il vous plaira me
pardonner si je me mets trop avant a vous donner conseil, car je le faicts pensant qu'il[?] cecy aidera fort a
maintenir [?vostre] bonne amitye; et aussi que je croys que [mm] a touché cela, s'asseurant que je le vous ferois
entendre. Quelcun m'a voulu dire que le filz aisné de [ϖ] retornera avec l'archeduc Charles, de quoy [ϖ] ne m'a riens dict; je ne faillray de m'en enquerir au vray et vous en adviser. Le second filz du duc de Bavieres ira aussi en Espaigne avec
ledict archeduc Charles.

## Notes

- f094 c12, c14, c21 "lele" before "faict"/"bien": read as "le" / "[?pour] le"; the doubled "le" recurs and may hide a
  code; not settled.
- f094 c19-c20 "deuerso leuy FAIRE entandre": the person (the King of Spain) is not carried; "[…]".
- f094v c05 "la?chelusiondu ET toutneuou LL ant" ("la conclusion du[dict] … et tout, ne voullant"): one word missing.
- f094v c17 "emlarcueragixes" = "embarquera a [?Gênes]": the port name is two signs ("gixes").
- f094v c18-c20 "ET eroict bien saoernaryrance" ("[?il] seroict bien [?aise de] passer par France"), "cuem ET uous"
  ("que [?…] et vous"): one lone sign before "et vous" (probably the King) not read.
- f095 c08 code 16 before "bonne amitye": read [?vostre] (fork B found 16 = vostre in the 1569 letters to the King);
  c09 lone mm ("QUE mm a touché cela") and c11 lone ϖ ("le filz aisné de [ϖ]", the Emperor's eldest son Rudolf, who
  was in Spain 1564-71) stand as codes.
- f095 c12 "decuoymnemari ET ysdict" ("de quoy … marri …"): not read.
- No image check in this pass.
Translation

# Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 14 August 1568 (cipher passages)

[f094] ⟨… in which you write to me⟩ that I should try to learn in detail what the couriers from Spain have brought,
which I have done as diligently as I could; of which, however, I have been able to learn nothing other than what I wrote
to you in my previous letters. You will have learnt from that of the [?sixth] of this month how the Emperor kept telling
me that he would speak to me. Since then he has told me that what he wanted to tell me was that he is sending his
brother Archduke Charles to Spain for two reasons: one, the affair of the Prince of Spain, and the other, the affairs of
Flanders, which seem to be in a state of great need of good provision, [?for] the good of the country, the peace of
Germany and of all Christendom; and that, holding the office he holds, he is bound to make every effort in it, which he
could make with no greater effect than by sending his brother himself to […], to make known to him his advice and what
all the princes of Germany judge necessary for the good of his country of Flanders. He will be very glad if the embassy
is found good and bears fruit, and if it does not profit, at the least he will be discharged before God and all

[f094v] the world of having done his duty and what belongs to a good brother. He also told me that on his said
brother's return he will be resolved on the matter of the marriage, for he will certainly come to the conclusion of
the said [?…], and all, not wishing on any account to wait longer; and although he has sent a courier ahead, he expects
no answer on this matter, because he says that up to now it has been handled by letters, which speak only once; but that
to be better satisfied and avoid delay and so many words, he has thought it better that his brother himself should
bring about the conclusion, which he hopes to have within four months, the length of his brother's journey; he will
leave on the fourth of this September to go, and will embark at Genoa. And that on his return he [?would like] very much to pass through France; to which I told him I would be very glad of it, for I was sure that [ϖ] (the King) and you would
take great pleasure in it, and in having occasion to welcome him and show the goodwill you have always borne and still
bear him, and all who depend on his majesty; for which reason I begged him earnestly to do me the favour of letting me
know, so that I could tell you. He told me he was not yet sure of the road he will take on his return, but that he
will not fail to

[f095] let me know. I think it would be well for you to write to him that you have heard from me that his brother was
going to Spain, and that if on his return he wished to pass through France you offer him the country and passage. You
will please forgive me if I go too far in giving you advice, for I do it thinking that this will greatly help to keep
[?your] good friendship; and also because I believe that [mm] touched on this, being sure that I would let you know it.
Someone has tried to tell me that the eldest son of [the Emperor] will return with Archduke Charles, about which [ϖ] (the Emperor) has told me nothing; I shall not fail to inquire into the truth of it and let you know. The second son of the Duke of Bavaria will also go
to Spain with the said Archduke Charles.

21 Aug 1568, to the Queen Mother (v103r)

Scipion de Fiesque, French envoy at the court of Emperor Maximilian II, to the Queen Mother, Vienna, 21 Aug 1568, during the negotiation of Charles IX's marriage with Elisabeth of Austria. Contents (conjectural, from the contributor's page list): Passage of Archduke Charles returning from Spain; Provence; the Count of Tende.

1568-08-21_reading.md

<!-- Reading of the cipher of Fiesque's letter to the Queen Mother, Vienna, 21 Aug 1568 (BnF fr. 15919, Gallica view 103 right), built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (reread batch b02). It rests on two texts that agree: the contemporary decipherment written in the left margin of the page (about 40 short lines in a secretary's hand, read from native-resolution openapi.bnf.fr IIIF crops), and the key decode of the contributed transcription (contributed/data/decoded_v103r.txt), aligned line by line (c01-c18). The margin is followed; the decode carries every clause. Spelling kept, abbreviations expanded, punctuation added. [?word] = partly carried (margin cramped and decode garbled at the same place); […] = not made out. -->

# Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 21 August 1568 (cipher passage)

[v103r] [Que…] qu'il sçavoit bien que offririez a l'empereur le pays et passaige a l'archeduc Charles son frere quand
il retourneroit d'Espaigne; et aussi que, se par advanture en y allant il touchoit a Marseille ou aultre lieu de
Provence, d'ordonner a monsieur le comte de Tende de luy faire le recueil et caresses qui appartiennent a tel prince.
Car l'empereur ayme fort ce frere, luy ayant esté tousjours obeissant, et mesmes a ceste derniere depesche, a laquelle
il s'est monstré fort prompt a sa volunté, […]nt que tous les honneurs et plaisirs qui luy seront faicts, qu'il les
reputera estre faicts a luy. Encores qu'il ne m'aye rien dict du passaige de son frere en France, par conjectures je
me suis apperceu que si vous le luy offrez, qu'il ne faillira de retourner par ce chemin, car l'empereur en a bonne
envye. Et [?l'on tient pour seur] qu'il partira le quatriesme de septembre.

## Alignment notes

- c01 decode "QUE secosc QUEL uureamlesass ET saoaigealarcheduccharles ET" against the margin "qu'il savoit bien que
  offririez a l'empereur le pays et passaige a l'archeduc Charles": the decode carries "qu'il … a … le [p]a[y]s et
  [p]assaige a l'archeduc Charles"; "sçavoit bien que offririez" and "l'empereur" rest on the margin (the decode has
  "secosc", "uure", "am": the opening of the cipher sentence is in the clear line above, not transcribed here).
- c03 "sesaraduuantuce" = "se par advanture" (margin "se par advanture"), y-shape read p.
- c07 "ce car m ayme fort se frere": the lone m (ϖ) is the ∞ code for l'empereur (margin "car l'empereur ayme fort ce
  frere"), as fork B found on views 109-168.
- c10-c11 "a uolunte oeu nt QUE": the margin has "a sa volunté" then a word not made out; left as "[…]nt".
- c15 "si VOUS leuuyoorescuiunefai LL irade" = "si vous le luy offrez qu'il ne faillira de" (margin "si vous luy offrez
  qu'il ne faillira de retourner que par ce chemin").
- c16 "car m enacoheenuye" = "car [l'empereur] en a bonne envye" (ϖ again; margin "car l'empereur y a bonne envye").
- c17 "lese?tinuetouseseurs QUEL sartir le cuatriesme de sestemcre": "qu'il partira le quatriesme de septembre" is sure
  in both; the clause before it is not made out in the margin, and the decode gives only "[?l'on tient pour seur]".

1568-08-21_en.md

<!-- English translation of read/1568-08-21_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026, batch b02). Same holes. -->

# Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 21 August 1568 (cipher passage)

[v103r] […] that he well knew you would offer the Emperor's brother, Archduke Charles, the country and passage when he
returned from Spain; and also that if by chance on his way there he touched at Marseille or some other place in
Provence, [you would] order the Count of Tende to give him the welcome and attentions that belong to such a prince. For
the Emperor loves this brother dearly, since he has always been obedient to him, and even in this last mission, in which
he showed himself very ready to do his will, […] that all the honours and pleasures done to the Archduke he will count
as done to himself. Although he has told me nothing about his brother's passage through France, I have gathered by
conjecture that if you offer it to him, he will not fail to return by that road, for the Emperor much wishes it. And
[?it is held for certain] that he will leave on the fourth of September.

4 Sep 1568 (docket "au Roy"; the cipher addresses "Madame"), f100, f100v, f101, f101v read in part

Scipion de Fiesque, French envoy at the court of Emperor Maximilian II, to the King, Vienna, 4 Sep 1568, during the negotiation of Charles IX's marriage with Elisabeth of Austria. Contents (conjectural, from the contributor's page list): The marriage negotiation; the death of the Spanish prince; News of the prince's death; the Emperor's mourning; the King's subjects lobby German princes; The Emperor says he knows of no solicitation from France so far; The affairs of Flanders may be settled gently.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (94% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

c21: P o fu a tri ptil o 3n -| ++ fx a T fx ++ fu -| vbar a T .Dao .Day tri // 3y b o v ptil 1 T b tri fx vbar
c22: // pi fx fu a // ptil C fx ptil 3n v b o v T fu fx tri o fx vbar v b ptil -| ++ 3y 3n o T a fx ++ 3n vbar // f vbar // 3n T
c23: b o C a o b 1 fx vbar =22 pi C o ptil -| // x // 1 fx vbar =10 3n o T a // H# o fu -| // 3n T b o T
c24: v // -| ++ C o vbar ++ fu T tri H# ptil -| T vd // fx L tri 3n tri 3n a f vbar =22 C fx fu a o 3n vbar -| a v -| tri 3n T a
c25: =10 =22 fu ++ ptil v // fu tri o v o 3n vbar // f b ptil =22 v H# fu -| b ++ s a -| fx ++ fu =ll o ptil -| // C o ptil -| T

# c10 の「receu」と、行頭の「Day」の後の平文は書いていない。
# c13 の「par」は無効字。c14 の「ffx」は f 2つに横棒の形（Tomokiyo の ff に横棒＝t）。
