Reading

1568-09-04k_reading.md

<!-- Reading of the cipher of Fiesque's letter of 4 Sep 1568, endorsed "au Roy" (the text addresses "Madame"; BnF fr. 15919, ff. 100r-101v, Gallica views 105 right, 106 left, 106 right, 107 left), built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (reread batch b02) from the key decode of the contributed transcription (contributed/data/decoded_f100.txt, decoded_f100v.txt, decoded_f101.txt, decoded_f101v.txt; Tomokiyo's key), word by word, reading through the known sign confusions (y-shape s/p; t-shape c/o; fu u/l; a/o both e). The contributor's line readings of f. 100r in contributed/docs/READING.en.md were used only as a check. No contemporary decipherment found beside it. [?word] = partly carried; [...] = not read. Signs standing alone where a person is meant are given as codes: [ϖ] (key m; by context a Majesty, the Emperor or the King), [pp] (key ff, inferred; by context the King of Spain), [ss]. Clear text around the cipher not transcribed here. Spelling kept, punctuation added. -->

# Fiesque, Vienna, 4 September 1568, endorsed "au Roy" (cipher passages)

[f100] [...] et dict [...] que l'homme que je vous avois envoyé estoict [?revenu], et que ne m'aviez envoyé responce plus tost, attendant que le [ϖ] fut bien guery, lequel avoict eu plus [de] vingt jours la fiebvre, mais qu'il se portoict bien quand mon homme en partit, qui fut le vingt [?huictiesme] du passé; il avoict desja huict jours que la fiebvre l'avoict laissé. Aussi que vous m'aviez escript que trouviez bon ce que [il] trouvoict bon, [?et] que [?vous] estiez bien aise qu'il [?demeurast] en ceste bonne volunté d'achever le mariage, en laquelle il vous trouverroit tousjours preste, desirant que ceste bonne amitié que vous avez ensemble se [...] estraigne avec une bonne alliance.

Il m'a dict qu'a ceste heure il n'y a plus aucune difficulté que le mariaige [ne se] face, car le prince d'Espaigne estoict mort; mais que [pp] desiroict d'estre mediateur [de] ce mariaige, en quoy il ne pouvoict nier de luy qu'il ne [...]; [et] esperoict que vous en seriez [con]tante. Je luy [?dis] que je croyois seurement que vous trouveriez bon que [ss] en fut le mediateur de la bonne amitié [?et] intelligence que aviez ensemble. Il [?dit] que ja vous luy aviez faict entendre que vous estiez en practique avec Sa Majesté de ce mariaige, affin de ne luy [...] celle de vostre intention, [...] l'alliance qu'avez ensemble, tenant son filz, et [...] amy. Sur cela il me [?dict] que bien tost il envoyeroict en Espaigne quelque [f100v] personne, et qu'il me parleroict apres; mais que le mariaige estoict [...], et que son frere l'archeduc Charles n'iroict poinct en Espaigne. Voila tout ce que j'ay peu tirer [?a] ceste heure de [luy].

Je me suis bien gardé de luy avoir riens dict [de ce] que m'aviez escript de la mort du prince d'Espaigne. Et pource que mon homme a [?rencontré] le courrier du duc d'Albe, qui portoict les certaines nouvelles de la mort dudict prince, [...] il en avoict [...] devers Italie, et y print ceste couleur que mon homme, qui estoit revenu, avoict tardé a estre despesché et me porter la seure guerison du [...]; et pource [...] de vous escripre comme [il] aura trouvé cest accident: il ne m'en a dict autre chose sinon que tous sommes subjects a cela, et que du jour au lendemain il fault que [...] ce pas. Mais en ceste court l'on en parle bien diversement, selon la fantaisie des hommes. [ϖ] porte le grand deuil avec toute sa chambre, et a commandé que toute sa court s'habille de deuil en robes courtes.

J'ay depuis dict a [ϖ] [...] que aulcuns des subjects du Roy, ne se contentans de l'edict que [ϖ] leur a faict et ne se asseurans de sa parole, tousjours machinent quelque entreprinse; et que vous aviez entendu que pour donner couleur a leurs desseings ilz ont envoyé en Allemaigne devers plusieurs princes [ϖ] les persuader et faire entendre [f101] plusieurs choses bien eslongnées de la verité; et que m'aviez commandé de luy faire entendre cecy, affin que de bonne heure il puisse donner ordre envers lesdicts princes, s'il y en a quelques ungs qui voullent adherer a leurs mauvais desseins; et mesmement que [ϖ] les traicte doulcement et maintient tous les edicts qu'il leur a octroyé. Il est vray qu'il a si bien pourveu et donné ordre par tous les endroits de son royaume qu'il n'a aucune paour que ce soit en leur puissance ne d'aultruy de luy faire aucun mal; mais que vous desiriez de retrancher tous les moyens qu'ilz puissent remettre les armes en ce royaulme.

Il m'a dict qu'il n'a sceu jusques a ceste heure que aucuns princes d'Allemaigne ayent esté sollicités du costé de France, mais qu'il en escripra a ses commissaires qui sont vers les [...], et fera toutes les provisions qu'il pourra qu'il ne soict presté oreille aux faulx rapports que l'on pourroict porter de France, et aussi qu'il ne soict donné aucun secours contre [ϖ]; mais qu'il vous conseille bien que vous evitiez toutes les occasions qui puissent [...] ce [...]; et encores qu'il soict asseuré que vous estes assez prudente, et que ce conseil [?est] si saige que vous ne mettrez aucune chose [...] l'establissement du repos de ce royaume, toutesfois [ϖ] l'envye qu'il a que vos affaires [...] que les siens propres, qu'il ne veult faillir de dire son advis.

Et me commençant a parler des affaires [f101v] de Flandres, [me] semble qu'il n'a esté creu de ce qu'il a tousjours conseillé, et qu'a ceste heure il s'en trouve en peine, et que du commencement l'on pouvoict remedier a plusieurs choses; qu'a ceste heure que le feu est allumé, il ne [...] si facilement il se pourra amortir. Je luy ay dict que, voyant la chrestienté en si grande extremité, que s'il vouloict mettre en avant quelque moyen pour le repos universel, et que [pp] y voulust condescendre, que je m'asseurois que [ϖ] de son costé ne s'espargneroict riens pour l'establissement du repos publicq, et qu'il se gouvernera selon son conseil, car [?il] l'ayme et revere comme son propre pere. Il m'a dict qu'il est asseuré de la bonne volunté du Roy, et que [...] moindre envers luy; mais que pour ceste heure il ne veoid que les affaires soient en terme qu'il s'en puisse esperer aucun bon fruict.

J'ay entendu de quelque bon lieu que la facherie que [ϖ] a est [?d'avoir] esté [?adverty] que les princes ne [...] aide ne envoyassent gens contre Flandres, les asseuroit qu'il [ϖ] moyenneroict que les affaires de Flandres se composeroient avec doulceur, et donnoict esperance que [pp] asseureroict de [?leurs] vies et biens; et que depuis l'on a veu le contraire, et semble que les princes s'en plaignent; de quoy il ne m'a riens touché. Mais quelqu'ung qui le peult sçavoir m'en a [...]

# Fiesque, Vienna, 4 September 1568, endorsed "au Roy" (cipher passages)

[f100] [...] and said [...] that the man I had sent you had [?returned], and that you had not sent me an answer sooner, waiting until [ϖ] was quite cured, who had had fever for more than twenty days, but was well when my man left, which was the [?twenty-eighth] of last month; the fever had already left him a week before. Also that you had written to me that you found good what [he] found good, and that you were very glad that he [?remained] in this good will to complete the marriage, in which he would always find you ready, wishing that this good friendship you have together should be [...] tightened with a good alliance.

He told me that now there is no longer any difficulty in the way of the marriage being made, since the Prince of Spain was dead; but that [pp] wished to be mediator [of] this marriage, in which he could not deny of himself that he did not [...]; [and] he hoped you would be content with it. I [?told] him that I believed for certain that you would find it good that [ss] should be the mediator of the good friendship and understanding you had together. He [?said] that you had already let him know that you were in negotiation with His Majesty about this marriage, so as not to [...] him that of your intention, [...] the alliance you have together, holding his son, and [...] friend. Thereupon he [?told] me that he would soon send some [f100v] person to Spain, and would speak to me afterwards; but that the marriage was [...], and that his brother the Archduke Charles would not go to Spain. That is all I have been able to draw from [him] at present.

I took good care not to tell him anything [of what] you had written to me of the death of the Prince of Spain. And because my man met the Duke of Alba's courier, who carried sure news of the death of the said prince, [...] he had [...] towards Italy, and took this pretext, that my man, who had come back, had been late in being dispatched and in bringing me the sure recovery of [...]; and therefore [...] to write to you how [he] will have taken this misfortune: he said nothing else to me about it except that we are all subject to it, and that from one day to the next one must [...] that step. But at this court it is spoken of very variously, according to men's fancy. [ϖ] wears full mourning with all his chamber, and has ordered all his court to dress in mourning in short robes.

Since then I have told [ϖ] [...] that some of the King's subjects, not content with the edict [ϖ] has made for them and not trusting his word, are always plotting some enterprise; and that you had heard that to give colour to their designs they have sent into Germany to several princes [ϖ] persuade them and give them to understand [f101] many things far from the truth; and that you had ordered me to make this known to him, so that in good time he might take order with the said princes, if there are any who wish to join in their evil designs; and especially that [ϖ] treats them gently and maintains all the edicts he has granted them. It is true that he has so well provided and given orders in every part of his kingdom that he has no fear that it is in their power or anyone else's to do him any harm; but that you wished to cut off every means by which they could take up arms again in this kingdom.

He told me he had not known until now that any princes of Germany had been solicited from the side of France, but that he will write about it to his commissioners who are with the [...], and will make every provision he can so that no ear is lent to the false reports that may be brought from France, and also that no help is given against [ϖ]; but that he strongly advises you to avoid every occasion that could [...] this [...]; and although he is sure that you are prudent enough, and that this council is so wise that you will put nothing [...] the establishment of the peace of this kingdom, still [ϖ] the wish he has that your affairs [...] than his own, that he will not fail to give his opinion.

And beginning to speak to me of the affairs [f101v] of Flanders, it seems to me that he was not believed in what he always advised, and that now he finds himself troubled by it, and that at the beginning many things could have been remedied; that now that the fire is lit, it will not [...] so easily be put out. I told him that, seeing Christendom in such great extremity, if he would put forward some means for the universal peace, and [pp] were willing to agree to it, I was sure that [ϖ] on his side would spare nothing for the establishment of the public peace, and that he would govern himself by his counsel, for he loves and reveres him as his own father. He told me he is assured of the King's good will, and that [...] less towards him; but that for the present he does not see affairs at a point where any good fruit can be hoped for.

I have heard from a good source that the vexation [ϖ] has is at having been informed that the princes [...] help nor send men against Flanders, he assured them that [ϖ] would arrange that the affairs of Flanders should be settled gently, and gave hope that [pp] would guarantee their lives and goods; and that since then the contrary has been seen, and it seems that the princes complain of it; of which he said nothing to me. But someone who can know it has [...] me [...]

4 Sep 1568, to the Queen Mother (v109r, v110l, v110r) read in part

Scipion de Fiesque, French envoy at the court of Emperor Maximilian II, to the Queen Mother, Vienna, 4 Sep 1568, during the negotiation of Charles IX's marriage with Elisabeth of Austria. Contents (conjectural, from the contributor's page list): The marriage; Archduke Ernst; Bohemia; The Emperor loves his eldest daughter; a good alliance would confirm the friendship; Keeping the edicts; he will find out whether the Archduke passes through France.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (94% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

# 4 Sep 1568, Scipion de Fiesque to the Queen Mother, Vienna (BnF fr. 15919, views 109 right, 110 left and right)

[v109r] Depuis vous avoir escript ceste premiere lettre, [∞ l'Empereur], qui m'avoict promis de me dire quelque chose de
sa volunté devant que l'ordinaire de Flandres partist, m'a dict que, ayant [∞?] envye d'estre moyenneur de ce mariaige
comme il m'avoict dict, il luy semble de le contanter, et qu'il croit que vous [?aussi] le trouverez bon, car il en
[?vauldra] mieulx; qu'il a resolu d'envoyer le seigneur Ernest […] en Espaigne, et de ce faict il a envoyé querir en
Boesme; et qu'il espere que [∞ le Roy] d'Espaigne […] le mariaige, car il escripra de façon qu'il y congnoistra qu'il a
envye que ce mariaige s'acheve le plus tost qu'il se [?pourra]. Je l'ay encores asseuré que je croyois que vous seriez
tres aise qu'il [?moyennast] ce mariage, et que n'aviez moings d'envye que luy de venir a la conclusion. Il m'a dict qu'il
n'y a plus aucun obstacle, mais [?me contanter] qu'il fault attendre encores ce peu de temps; et qu'encores que le
prince d'Espaigne ne fut mort, que son intention estoict tousjours […] qu'il [eust] alliance avec [∞ le Roy] d'une de ses
filles; et que si par les […] occasions on [?a] apporté quelques difficultés, qu'il ne les estimoict telles qu'elles
[?deussent] empescher ceste bonne oeuvre; et que l'evesque de Retes mesmes, qui avoict demeuré long temps

[v110l] aupres de luy, a […] fort bien seu congnoistre la bonne affection qu'il vous portoict tousjours. Lors j'ay
prins occasion de luy dire que, estant asseuré l'evesque de Retes de la bonne amityé qu'il portoict au Roy, il jugea
de ne [?porter] l'escript, qu'il sçait [ϖ] ne donner aucune occasion d'alterer leur bonne amityé, se persuadant que
quelques uns avoient mis en avant cest empeschement [?pour] separer leur conjunction; mais que a la fin ceste amityé se
confirmeroict par une bonne alliance. Il m'a dict qu'il ayme fort sa fille aisnée, et plus que tous ses enfans, et qu'il
espere que vous et [∞ le Roy] en demeurerez contans; et me le disoict de sorte que l'on voyoit bien dedans son cueur la
joye qu'il avoict de la veoir royne de France. Je l'ay asseuré que [∞ le Roy] et vous vous porteriez en l'endroict
d'elle de maniere que tous les jours il en sera plus contant de ce mariaige. Il m'a dict derechef qu'il escripra a ses
commissaires qu'ilz prennent garde qu'il ne se face quelques faulx rapports [?aux] princes, et que de leurs pays ne se
lieve gens contre [∞ le Roy]; et que je vous escripvisse qu'il vous conseille et prie bien fort de maintenir […] en vostre
royaume. Je luy ay dict que [∞ le Roy]

[v110r] faict a tout son pouvoir, mais qu'il y en [a] aulcuns qui ne se contantent de la raison, […] que [ϖ] [?plus] asseure tous [?les] jours de maintenir ses edicts. […] le seigneur Ernest […] de [?…] et chevalier de la Toison il sera arrivé, je tacheray de sçavoir s'il passera par France, [ϖ] vous en advisera affin que [?…] caresser comme son estat merite.

# 4 Sep 1568, Scipion de Fiesque to the Queen Mother, Vienna

[v109r] Since I wrote you this first letter, [the Emperor], who had promised to tell me something of his will before the
ordinary courier of Flanders left, told me that, [?] having the wish to be the mediator of this marriage as he had told
me, he thinks fit to satisfy him, and that he believes that you [?too] will find it good, for it will [?be] the better
for it; that he has resolved to send Lord Ernest […] to Spain, and for this purpose he has sent for him in Bohemia; and
that he hopes that [the King] of Spain […] the marriage, for he will write in such a way that he will see that he wishes
this marriage to be concluded as soon as it [?can]. I assured him again that I believed you would be very glad that he
[?should mediate] this marriage, and that you had no less wish than he to come to the conclusion. He told me that there
is no longer any obstacle, but [?] that one must still wait this little time; and that even if the prince of Spain had
not died, his intention was always […] that he should [have] an alliance with [the King] through one of his daughters;
and that if, on the […] occasions, some difficulties had been raised, he did not consider them such as [?ought] to
hinder this good work; and that the Bishop of Retes himself, who had stayed a long time

[v110l] with him, has […] very well been able to see the good affection he always bore you. Then I took the occasion to tell him
that, the Bishop of Retes being assured of the good friendship he bore the King, he judged not to [?carry] the writing, that he knows [ϖ] (the King) gives no occasion to alter their good friendship, persuading himself that some people had put
forward this obstacle to separate their union; but that in the end this friendship would be confirmed by a good
alliance. He told me that he loves his eldest daughter dearly, more than all his children, and that he hopes that you
and [the King] will be content with her; and he said it in such a way that one could see in his heart the joy he had in
seeing her Queen of France. I assured him that [the King] and you would behave towards her in such a way that every day
he will be more content with this marriage. He told me again that he will write to his commissioners that they take
care that no false reports are made [?to the] princes, and that no troops are raised in their lands against [the King];
and that I should write to you that he advises and begs you very strongly to maintain […] in your kingdom. I told him
that [the King]

[v110r] is doing all in his power, but that there are some who are not content with reason, […] that [ϖ] (the King) [?more] assures [?them] every day that he will maintain his edicts. […] Lord Ernest […] of [?…] and knight of the Golden Fleece has arrived, I will try to find out whether he will pass through France; [ϖ] will let you know, so that [?…] welcome him as his rank deserves.

6 Nov 1568, to the Queen Mother (v125r)

Scipion de Fiesque, French envoy at the court of Emperor Maximilian II, to the Queen Mother, Vienna, 6 Nov 1568, during the negotiation of Charles IX's marriage with Elisabeth of Austria. Contents (conjectural, from the contributor's page list): Troops for the Prince of Condé; the King of Denmark; Orange; Alba.

<!-- Reading of the cipher of Fiesque's letter to the Queen Mother, Vienna, 6 Nov 1568 (BnF fr. 15919, Gallica view 125 right), built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (reread batch b02). It rests on the key decode of the contributed transcription (contributed/data/decoded_v125r.txt, c01-c14), which reads almost straight, and on the contemporary decipherment written in the left margin of the page (about 35 short lines, read from native-resolution openapi.bnf.fr IIIF crops). The margin agrees clause by clause and supplies the opening (the decode's c01 is garbled). Spelling kept, punctuation added. [?word] = partly carried; […] = not made out. -->

# Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 6 November 1568 (cipher passage)

[v125r] Le bruict court que le duc Wolfgang de Deux Pontz [?a assemblé] environ six mil reistres et troys regiments de
gens de pied pour envoyer au secours de monsieur le prince de Condé; et que le roy de Dannemarc espouse la soeur du
prince d'Aurenges, et qu'il faict une ligue avec toutes les villes maritimes pour envoyer secours audict prince
d'Aurenges. J'ay entendu de bon lieu qu'il y a long temps que l'Allemaigne n'estoit si unie comme elle est a ceste heure
pour deffendre la querelle dudict prince d'Aurenges. Les commissaires ont continué leur voyaige vers le duc d'Albe et
prince d'Aurenges; mais la plus grand partie croid qu'ilz n'en proffiteront riens.

## Alignment notes

- Margin, first lines: "Le bruict court / que le duc / Wolfgang de Deux / Pontz [?] / [?] mil / reistres et troys /
  regimens de gens / de pied pour envoyer / au secours de mons.r / le prince de Condé". The decode c01 has only
  "deux sont snbcaoemiceueuent sie milue" ("Deux [P]ont[z] … six mil re[istres]"): "le duc Wolfgang de" is in the clear
  line before the cipher or rests on the margin; the verb and "environ" are not made out in either ([?…]).
- H# read ss in "commi[ss]aires" (c11), as in OPEN.en.md §7; pp = ff in "pro[ff]iteront" (c13).
- Margin end: "… prince d'Aurenges mais / la plus grand part / croid qu'ilz n'y / profiteront / rien": the margin
  abbreviates the commissaires sentence, which is read from the decode (c10-c14 read straight).
Translation

1568-11-06_en.md

<!-- English translation of read/1568-11-06_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026, batch b02). Same holes. -->

# Fiesque to Catherine de' Medici, Vienna, 6 November 1568 (cipher passage)

[v125r] The rumour runs that Duke Wolfgang of Zweibrücken [?has gathered] some six thousand reiters and three regiments
of foot to send to the help of the Prince of Condé; and that the King of Denmark is marrying the sister of the Prince of
Orange, and that he is making a league with all the maritime towns to send help to the said Prince of Orange. I have
heard from a good source that Germany has not for a long time been as united as it is now to defend the quarrel of the
said Prince of Orange. The commissioners have continued their journey towards the Duke of Alba and the Prince of Orange;
but most people believe they will achieve nothing.

2 Dec 1568 (Speyer), to the Queen Mother (v129r, v130l)

Scipion de Fiesque, French envoy at the court of Emperor Maximilian II, to the Queen Mother, Speyer, 2 Dec 1568 (Speyer), during the negotiation of Charles IX's marriage with Elisabeth of Austria. Contents (conjectural, from the contributor's page list): The Duke of Aumale; levies of reiters; Pretext of defending the Empire's lands; all to be settled on Archduke Charles's return.

Transcription

# 2 Dec 1568, Scipion de Fiesque to the Queen Mother, Speyer (BnF fr. 15919, views 129 right, 130 left)

[v129r] [∞ L'Empereur] m'a dict qu'il luy a esté escript que monsieur d'Aumale s'en alloict assieger une ville du duc
Wulfhang de Bavieres, et que si cela estoict vray, qu'il craignoict que tous les cercles de l'Empire s'en
ressentiroient et ne remuassent chose qui pourroict bien retourner au dommaige du Roy. Je luy ay [dict] que je pensois
que si monsieur d'Aumale estoict allé aux terres du duc Wulfang, que ce qu'il aura faict [a esté] pour le divertir qu'il
ne menast en France contre [∞ le Roy] ce [?secours] que le bruict couroict qu'il assembloict. Il m'a dict qu'il
trouvoict bien bon qu'on empeschast que aucunes forces n'allassent contre [∞ le Roy], qu'il se debvoict mettre bon ordre
et diligence en ce faict; mais que d'aller assieger les terres appartenantes a l'Empire, il doubte que ce ne soict
occasion qu'ilz

[v130l] [se] resouldre ceulx qui demeurent quoys; ce qu'il de mesme fict entendre au duc [?d'Albe], a ce que je puis
comprandre. [∞ L'Empereur], qui a faict tous les bons offices qu'il a peu envers les princes d'Allemaigne affin qu'ilz ne
donnassent ayde a autre […] ne voul[oit] qu'ilz prinssent occasion de s'assembler soubz couleur de deffendre les pays de
l'Empire. Je vous ay escript comme il avoict faict entendre au legat Commendon qu'il ne s'innoveroit aucune chose
touchant la religion, et qu'il avoict donné congé aux deputés qui se debvoient assembler a la sainct Martin; et que
quant au faict du mariaige, il ne s'en parlera jusques a ce qu'il aye eu nouvelles de l'archeduc Charles. Je crois
qu'aurez peu descouvrir du costé d'Espaigne quelle est l'intention du [=ii], [?et] qu'il [vous] plaira m'advertir comme [?je]
me doibs gouverner, car je vois bien qu'il y a du changement. Toutesfois, a ce que je puis juger, l'intention de
[∞ l'Empereur] est que si [pp] espouse sa fille aisnée, que [∞ le Roy] espouse aussi la seconde. Je pense qu'au retour
de l'archeduc le tout sera resoulu.

# 2 Dec 1568, Scipion de Fiesque to the Queen Mother, Speyer

[v129r] [The Emperor] told me that he has been written to that Monsieur d'Aumale was going to besiege a town of Duke
Wolfgang of Bavaria, and that if this was true, he feared that all the circles of the Empire would resent it and would
stir up something that might well turn to the King's harm. I [told] him that I thought that if Monsieur d'Aumale had
gone to the lands of Duke Wolfgang, what he will have done [was] to divert him, so that he should not lead into France
against [the King] the [?force] which rumour said he was assembling. He told me that he thought it very good that any
forces should be prevented from going against [the King], that good order and diligence should be used in this; but that
as for besieging lands belonging to the Empire, he fears it may give occasion to

[v130l] those who stay quiet to [make up their minds]; which he likewise gave Duke [?of Alba] to understand, as far as I can
make out. [The Emperor], who has done every good office he could with the princes of Germany so that they should give no
help to another […] did not want them to take occasion to assemble under colour of defending the lands of the Empire.
I wrote to you how he had given the legate Commendone to understand that nothing would be innovated touching religion,
and that he had dismissed the deputies who were to assemble at Martinmas; and that as for the marriage, nothing will be
said of it until he has had news from Archduke Charles. I believe you will have been able to find out on the side of
Spain what the intention of [=ii] is, and that it will please you to advise me how I am to conduct myself, for I see
well that there is a change. Nevertheless, as far as I can judge, the intention of [the Emperor] is that if [pp] (the King of Spain) marries
his eldest daughter, [the King] should marry the second. I think that on the Archduke's return all will be settled.

14 Jan 1569, to the Queen Mother (v140r, v141l)

Scipion de Fiesque, French envoy at the court of Emperor Maximilian II, to the Queen Mother, Vienna, 14 Jan 1569, during the negotiation of Charles IX's marriage with Elisabeth of Austria. Contents (conjectural, from the contributor's page list): Courier from Madrid; the Cardinal of Guise; Barcelona and Zaragoza; the King of Portugal; The Queen of England; marriage of the Elector of Saxony's daughter.

# 14 Jan 1569, Scipion de Fiesque to the Queen Mother, Vienna (BnF fr. 15919, views 140 right, 141 left)

[v140r] Il [a] arrivé mercredy ung courrier que l'archeduc Charles a envoyé d'Espaigne, lequel partit de Madrid le
dixiesme du mois passé. J'ay dextrement tasché d'entendre de [∞ l'Empereur] s'il estoict encores faict aucune
deliberation touchant le mariaige. Il m'a dict que ce courrier ne portoict que l'arrivée de son frere, et que l'on
[…] au commancement des affaires; mais que bientost il en attandoict ung autre, par lequel il esperoict avoir quelque
lumiere; et que ce courrier avoict rencontré monsieur le cardinal de Guise entre Barcellone et Saragosse. Toutesfois,
encores que je l'aye sondé, disant que je desirois que le mariaige s'accomplist bientost, et que j'esperois, a ceste
heure que monsieur le cardinal de Guise est en Espaigne, que sans plus retarder tout se pourroit conclure, je n'ay sceu
en tirer aucun mot davantaige touchant ce faict. Le bruit court fort icy que [=ii, the King of Spain] espouse la fille
aisnée de [∞ l'Empereur]; et mesmes quelqu'ung qui sçait souvent [les] choses secretes m'a dict [que] ce mariaige du
[=ii] ce fera seurement, mais que [=ii] est apres que [∞ le Roy] espouse la seconde fille de [∞ l'Empereur], et
que madame Marguerite, [?vostre] fille, se marie au roy de Portugal; ce que

[v141l] vous sçaurez mieulx que je ne le pourrois escripre. Comme je vous ay escript par plusieurs fois, que vous en [?pouviez] attandre la resolution [?tout tost] du costé d'Espaigne, que de deça il me semble que tout despend de la
volunté du [=ii, the King of Spain]. [∞ L'Empereur] m'a dict que le duc Wolfang a en environ trois mille ensemble, et
que le bruit court que la royne d'Angleterre faisoit trois mille, mais que jusques a ceste heure il n'en a riens de
certain. Il me semble a son parler qu'il doubte que a ce printemps ne se descouvre une grande guerre. Les ambassadeurs
du [=ii, the Landgrave?] de Hesse sont icy, mais je n'ay peu entandre ce qu'ilz traictent. Le filz aisné du comte [?]
a ung filz de la soeur du [=ii]; et celluy qui m'avoict dict qu'il n'avoict point d'enfans est ung personnaige de grande
auctorité, qui me le disoict, s'estonnant que l'Electeur de Saxe [?donnast] sa fille en mariaige au Casimire.

<!-- English translation of read/1569-01-14_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026). Same holes. -->

# 14 Jan 1569, Scipion de Fiesque to the Queen Mother, Vienna

[v140r] On Wednesday a courier arrived whom Archduke Charles sent from Spain, who left Madrid on the tenth of last
month. I tactfully tried to learn from [the Emperor] whether any decision had yet been taken touching the marriage. He
told me that this courier brought only news of his brother's arrival, and that […] at the beginning of the business;
but that he soon expected another, by whom he hoped to have some light; and that this courier had met the Cardinal of
Guise between Barcelona and Zaragoza. However, although I sounded him, saying that I wished the marriage to be
accomplished soon, and that I hoped, now that the Cardinal of Guise is in Spain, that everything could be concluded
without further delay, I could not draw a word more out of him on this matter. The rumour runs strongly here that [the
King of Spain] will marry the eldest daughter of [the Emperor]; and even someone who often knows secret things told me
that this marriage of [the King of Spain] will certainly be made, but that [he] is working so that [the King] should
marry the second daughter of [the Emperor], and that Madame Marguerite, [?your] daughter, should marry the King of
Portugal; which

[v141l] you will know better than I could write it. As I have written to you several times, that you [?could] expect the decision [?very soon] from the side of Spain, since on this side it seems to me that everything depends on the will of [the
King of Spain]. [The Emperor] told me that Duke Wolfgang has about three thousand [?men] together, and that the rumour
runs that the Queen of England was raising three thousand, but that so far he has nothing certain. It seems to me from
his talk that he fears that a great war may break out this spring. The ambassadors of the [?Landgrave] of Hesse are here,
but I have not been able to learn what they are negotiating. The eldest son of Count [?] has a son by the sister of the
[=ii]; and the man who had told me that he had no children is a person of great authority, who told me so, wondering
that the Elector of Saxony should give his daughter in marriage to Casimir.

# 2 Mar 1569, Scipion de Fiesque to the Queen Mother, Vienna (BnF fr. 15919, view 146 right)

[v146r] [∞ L'Empereur] m'a dict qu'il a eu d'aultres nouvelles d'Allemaigne, par lesquelles [il] est advisé [con]me monsieur d'Aumale [a] bruslé et saccagé tout aupres de Saverne, ce que l'on semble fort estrange. Je luy ay dict que je n'en
crois riens, et que ce sont nouvelles controuvées de […] qui craignent, jugeans leurs consciences par les offences qu'ilz
ont faict au Roy; et que je m'asseure que le Roy ne permettra qu'il se face aucun dommaige aux pays de l'Empire, [con]me monsieur de la Forest a eu charge de luy faire entandre. Il m'a dict qu'il [a] faict doubler tous les advis qu'il
a receus pour les vous bailler, mais je crois que vous ne les [?pourrons] avoir jusques a demain; et partant
l'ordinaire, je n'ay voullu faillir [?vous] advertir de cecy. Au demeurant il ne s'entend riens de nouveau, sinon que
les Allemans monstrent avoir grande crainte de l'armée que le Roy a sur les frontieres; et n'est venu aucun courrier
d'Espaigne. Je crains que l'archeduc ne sera de retour de trois ⟨…⟩

1569-03-02_en.md

<!-- English translation of read/1569-03-02_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026). Same holes. -->

# 2 Mar 1569, Scipion de Fiesque to the Queen Mother, Vienna

[v146r] [The Emperor] told me that he has had other news from Germany, by which [he] is informed how Monsieur d'Aumale
[has] burnt and sacked everything near Saverne, which [people] find very strange. I told him that I believe none of it, and
that this is news invented by […] who are afraid, judging by their own consciences from the offences they have done the
King; and that I am sure that the King will not allow any harm to be done to the lands of the Empire, as Monsieur de la Forest was charged to give him to understand. He told me that he has had all the reports he has received copied, to
give them to you, but I believe you will [?not be able] to have them before tomorrow; and as the ordinary
courier is leaving, I did not want to fail to inform [?you] of this. For the rest, nothing new is heard, except that the
Germans show great fear of the army that the King has on the frontiers; and no courier has come from Spain. I fear that
the Archduke will not be back for three ⟨…⟩

10 Sep 1569, to the King (v149r, v150l, v150r) decoded, not read

Scipion de Fiesque, French envoy at the court of Emperor Maximilian II, to the King, Vienna, 10 Sep 1569, during the negotiation of Charles IX's marriage with Elisabeth of Austria. Contents (conjectural, from the contributor's page list): The Cardinal of Guise in Spain; the late Queen's death; resolution on the marriage; The ambassador to send within the month; Princess Marguerite and the King of Portugal; Help from Germany; the Empress's letter; wish for an early marriage.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

# 2026-10-20：この手では、h に似た尾つきの形（ℌ）が n（3n）、3 に似た尾つきの形（ʒ）が y（3y）。初めは逆に名付けていたので、c01–c19 を入れ替えた（入れ替えで envoyer・Espaigne・entendre などが読める）。三角（▲）は Δ（tri＝i）で、丸い a（e）と別。初めは a と書いていたので、c01–c17 を画像で見直して直した。
# 2026-10-20：c20–c30 を書き起こした（an_L20–an_L30）。ページの終わりまで。c29 の頭の大きな記号（A に長い縦画）は名付けられず ? とした。
# 2026-10-20（公開の初版の後の修正）：独立の2回目の2回がそろって食い違った箇所を、別々の読み手（Codex と Claude Sonnet、のちに Sonnet 2人）が画像で確かめ、読み手がそろって独立の2回目の読みを選んだ箇所だけを直した。前に直した箇所も、取り直した Sonnet が支えない所は元の読みに戻した（一覧は second/check/decisions*.tsv）。

# 暗号はここまで（ao_L22 の行末に無効字「Il ti ol」、続きは平文）。この手のʒ類：3 に似た尾つき（ʒ）は 3y（y）、ループつきの y 形は、文脈で v（s）か 3p（p）とした（形では分けられない。f.39r の y 形と同じ扱い）。初め y 形も 3y と書いていたので、c01–c06 を文脈で直した（par・expres・despescheray・cependant・peine・prier の p）。ŧŧ（H#）は c19「po(ss)ible」で ss と読める。

# 10 Sep 1569, Scipion de Fiesque to the King, Vienna (BnF fr. 15919, views 149 right, 150 left and right)

[v149r] ⟨…⟩ que je debvois dire et remonstrer, ayant icelluy retardé en chemin pour estre ce courrier tumbé malade a
Solleurre, qui a esté cause que monsieur de Bellievre me l'a envoyée par ung des [?saultiers] ordinaires du [?dict] lieu, affin
qu'elle me fut apportée seurement. Suivant laquelle, apres avoir donné vostre lettre, ensemble celle de la Royne, […],
je luy ay dict que m'aviez [?commandé] de luy faire entendre que, quand envoyastes monseigneur le cardinal de Guise en
Espaigne [?pour] [?se] condouloir de la mort de la feue royne vostre soeur, luy [?commandastes] d'entandre du [=ii] quelle
estoict sa resolution sur le faict du mariaige qui se traictoit de vostredicte majesté et madame sa fille aisnée, lequel
de par avant [?il] avoict monstré avoir fort agreable, et declairé vouloir estre le mediateur et moyenneur; [?et que]
icelluy auroict faict responce audict sieur cardinal que les choses estoient changées par la mort de la royne sa femme,
et que, estant veuf, il avoict deliberé d'espouser sadicte fille aisnée, pour estre d'aage plus convenable a luy, et
qu'il auroict fort agreable et desireroit que vostredicte majesté fut contante d'espouser la seconde; sur ce, ayant
entandu despuis vostre intention, vous auroict prié de n'en riens mander a sa majesté de deça que premierement il n'y
eust envoyé ung courrier, car il s'asseuroit que tout le faict de ce mariaige seroit remis sur luy; et que despuis il
vous auroit faict entandre par monsieur de Fourquevaulx, vostre ambassadeur, qu'il luy avoict remis l'entiere
resolution du mariage de sa seconde fille, lequel il desiroit estre conclud et traicté devant luy en Espaigne; ce
qu'ayant vostre majesté entandu, elle auroit envoyé ample pouvoir audict sieur de Fourquevaulx de ce faire, et icelluy

[v150l] [?conclurre], encores que l'intention d'icelle fut de [?le veoir faire] de deça en la court de sa majesté; et qu'aurez
[?sceu que] ledict sieur de Fourquevaulx vous auroit faict entandre que l'ambassadeur de sadicte majesté luy auroict dict
qu'elle avoict deliberé d'envoyer dans ce moys de septembre ses deux filles a [?Gennes] pour s'embarquer, [?et] que madame
Elysabeth, vostre future espouse, demourast a Marseille, ou madame Marguerite, vostre soeur, s'embarqueroict pour aller
en Espaigne avec sa fille aisnée, [?et] de la tireroit apres en Portugal; de quoy vostredicte majesté avoit eu grand
plaisir, pour le desir qu'elle a que son mariaige se effectuast le plus tost que il se pourroit; luy faisant entandre que
n'aviez envoyé personnaige de qualité pour demander sa fille, ainsi qu'il s'est tousjours observé en semblables choses,
pour [con]tanter [H#], lequel vous asseuroict que telle estoict sa volunté. Il m'a dict qu'il n'a pas tenu a luy que les mariages ne se soient accomplis, lesquelz il tient pour faictz, et que ceste longueur n'est [?de] sa coulpe, mais que [ffx-3y] n'a jamais escript resoluement de la [con]clusion, ains a tousjours [?proposé] quelques [con]ditions, lesquelles il falloit resouldre devant que de partir ses filles; qu'il luy despeschera
maintenant ung courrier pour [?cest] effect, jusques au retour duquel il ne veult prandre resolution sur ledict partement; toutesfois qu'il pense que
le tout, le moys de novembre au plus tard, le tout en sera resolu, [?et] qu'il vous envoyera bientost ung de ses
gentilzhommes [pour] [vous] faire [?entendre] la bonne volunté qu'il [?vous] porte; me promettant de me dire en confiance d'ou
provient icelle longueur qu'a esté cause du retardement de l'accomplissement des mariages, ce que j'attandray le veoir,
ce qu'il me dira; de quoy, si je veois estre chose

[v150r] d'importance, je ne faillray advertir vostredicte majesté par homme expres, que luy despescheray. Cependant j'ay
voullu envoyer ledict [?saultier] avec la presente, affin qu'elle ne demourast en peine. […] je ne suis voullu entrer en
propos avec [∞ l'Empereur] de ce qu'il vous a pleu [?me] commander par vostredicte instruction touchant quelque traicté [?pour]
la conservation de [?vostre] estat, par ce que je suis asseuré que ce eust esté vous descouvrir sans en tirer aucun fruict;
[?aussi] de ne le prier de donner ordre qu'il n'aille aucun secours d'Allemaigne en faveur de vos subjectz eslevés, car
ce ne seroit que temps perdu, l'ayant par plusieurs fois tasté [sur] cest effect, [?veu] clairement qu'il ne vouldroict
hazarder sa reputation, attandu qu'il cognoist qu'a grand peine seroict il obey. Estant tout ce que je vous puis
escripre pour ceste fois, sinon que j'ay aussi donné vostre lettre a l'Imperatrice, a laquelle j'ay aussi faict entandre
de vostre part le contantement qu'avez d'espouser sa fille, et que luy serez a jamais bon filz, la priant de tenir la
main a ce que le mariage se effectue le plus tost qu'il seroict possible; laquelle m'a dict qu'elle vous remercye, et que
[?pour] plusieurs raisons elle est tenue de ce [?faire] envers vous, a quoy elle s'employera de bien bon coeur, [?et] vous
fera responce, laquelle elle me baillera apres ⟨…⟩

# 10 Sep 1569, Scipion de Fiesque to the King, Vienna

[v149r] ⟨…⟩ which I was to say and represent, it having been delayed on the way because this courier fell ill at
Solothurn, which was the reason why Monsieur de Bellièvre sent it to me by one of the ordinary [?couriers] of the [?said] place, so that it
should be brought to me safely. In accordance with it, after delivering your letter together with the Queen's, […], I told
him that you had [?ordered] me to let him know that, when you sent Monseigneur the Cardinal of Guise to Spain to [?offer]
condolences on the death of the late Queen your sister, you [?ordered] him to learn from [the King of Spain] what his
decision was on the marriage being negotiated between Your said Majesty and Madame his [the Emperor's] eldest daughter,
which he had earlier shown he found very agreeable, and declared he wished to be its mediator and go-between; [?and that]
he had answered the said Cardinal that things were changed by the death of the Queen his wife, and that, being a widower,
he had decided to marry the said eldest daughter, as being of an age more suitable to him, and that he would find it very
agreeable and would wish Your said Majesty to be content to marry the second; thereupon, having since learnt your
intention, he had begged you to send nothing of it to His Majesty here until he had first sent a courier, for he was sure
that the whole matter of this marriage would be left to him; and that since then he had let you know through Monsieur de
Fourquevaux, your ambassador, that he [the Emperor] had left to him the entire decision on the marriage of his second
daughter, which he wished to be concluded and negotiated before him in Spain; which Your Majesty having heard, you had
sent ample powers to the said Sieur de Fourquevaux to do this, and he

[v150l] [?to conclude it], although your intention was [?to see it done] here at His Majesty's court; and that you will [?have learnt
that] the said Sieur de Fourquevaux had let you know that His said Majesty's ambassador had told him that he had decided
to send his two daughters this month of September to [?Genoa] to take ship, and that Madame Elisabeth, your future wife,
should stay at Marseille, where Madame Marguerite, your sister, would embark to go to Spain with his eldest daughter, and
from there would go on to Portugal; at which Your said Majesty had been very pleased, for the wish you have that your
marriage should take effect as soon as possible; letting him know that you had not sent a person of rank to ask for his
daughter, as has always been observed in such matters, in order to [satisfy] [H#], who assured you that such was his will. He told me
that it has not been his fault that the marriages have not been accomplished, which he holds as done, and that this delay is not [?of] his fault, but that [ffx-3y] has never written resolutely about the conclusion, but has always [?proposed] some conditions, which had to be settled before his daughters left; that he will now send him a courier [?for this] purpose, until whose return he will not take a decision on the said
departure; nevertheless he thinks that the whole will be settled in the month of November at the latest, and that he
will soon send you one of his gentlemen to let you know the good will he bears you; promising to tell me in confidence
where this delay comes from which has caused the postponement of the marriages, which I shall wait to see, what he will
tell me; of which, if I see it to be a matter

[v150r] of importance, I shall not fail to inform Your said Majesty by a special messenger, whom I shall send. Meanwhile I
wanted to send the said [?courier] with this letter, so that you should not remain in anxiety. […] I did not want to
enter into talk with [the Emperor] about what you were pleased to command me by your said instruction concerning some treaty [?for] the preservation of your state, because I am sure that it would have been to reveal yourself without
drawing any fruit from it; [?also] not to ask him to give orders that no help should go from Germany in favour of your
risen subjects, for it would only be time lost, having sounded him several times [on] this point, [?seeing] clearly that
he would not wish to hazard his reputation, given that he knows that he would hardly be obeyed. This being all that I can write to you for this time, except that I have also given your letter to the Empress, to whom I have also conveyed on
your behalf the contentment you have in marrying her daughter, and that you will forever be a good son to her, begging
her to lend a hand so that the marriage may take effect as soon as possible; she told me that she thanks you, and that
for several reasons she is bound to [?do] this towards you, in which she will employ herself very willingly, and will
answer you, and will give me the answer afterwards ⟨…⟩

1 Oct 1569 (date conjectural), to the Queen Mother (v155r)

# 1 Oct 1569 (date conjectural), Scipion de Fiesque to the Queen Mother, Vienna (BnF fr. 15919, view 155 right)

[v155r] Vous aurez peu veoir estre amplement esclaircye par celle que vous ay envoyée par mon homme, que despeschay le

pouvant a present escripre autre chose, fors que je sçay au vray que messieurs de Chantonnay et Vanega ont faict grande instance […] pour faire avancer les mariages, lesquelz toutesfois n'auroient encores riens sceu resouldre, sinon qu'on attendra le
courrier que je vous ay escript que [∞ l'Empereur] a dernierement despesché en Espaigne, lequel ne sçauroict estre plus
tost qu'a la sainct Martin prochaine de retour. J'ay dict a [∞ l'Empereur] ce que m'avez escript de l'estat des [?affaires] du [ϖ], dont il a esté fort aise, mesmement que vos forces soient ensemble; lequel m'a dict qu'il a eu nouvelles que vos
ennemys ont levé le siege de devant Poictiers, et desire d'entandre quel chemin ilz ont prins; aussi qu'il n'a advis qu'il
se face aucune levée de gens en Allemaigne; toutesfois qu'il est advisé qu'il y a plusieurs intelligences, desquelles
jusques a ceste heure il n'en est riens descouvert. Il monstre avoir grand desir de veoir [∞ le Roy] hors de ces troubles.

# 1 Oct 1569 (date conjectural), Scipion de Fiesque to the Queen Mother, Vienna

[v155r] You will have been able to see fully explained, in the letter I sent you by my man whom I dispatched on
the thirteenth of last month, what you write to me in yours of the twenty-eighth of the month of August last; of which,
being unable to write anything else at present, except that I know for certain that Messieurs de Chantonnay and Vanega (Venegas) have pressed hard […] to have the marriages advanced, who however have not yet been able to settle anything, except that the
courier will be awaited whom, as I wrote to you, [the Emperor] lately sent to Spain, who cannot be back before next
Martinmas. I told [the Emperor] what you wrote to me of the state of the [?affairs] of [ϖ] (the King), at which he was very glad, especially
that your forces are together; he told me that he has had news that your enemies have raised the siege before Poitiers,
and wishes to learn what road they have taken; also that he has no report of any levy of men being made in Germany;
nevertheless he is informed that there are several intrigues, of which nothing has so far been discovered. He shows a
great wish to see [the King] out of these troubles.

15 Oct 1569 (date conjectural), to the Queen Mother (v161r)

# 15 Oct 1569 (date conjectural), Scipion de Fiesque to the Queen Mother, Vienna

[v161r] ⟨…⟩ except that I asked [the Emperor] to tell me whether he had learnt that anything is being negotiated [at]
the assembly being held at Erfurt that might turn to the prejudice of [ϖ] (the King). He told me that he has heard nothing more
than what he had lately told me, except that the Queen of England's ambassador is persuading the Protestant princes as
hard as he can not to abandon your enemies, and to help them, promising that she will do the same on her side; but that
he has no report that any stirring of arms is yet being made in Germany, where he has given good orders to be informed of
whatever is stirred there.

20 Oct 1569, to the King (v159r) read in part

# 20 Oct 1569, Scipion de Fiesque to the King, Vienna (BnF fr. 15919, view 159 right)

[v159r] ⟨… a ceste guerre.⟩ Et depuis qu'il a entandu que les [?p]rotestans et plusieurs aultres de leur
religion font une assem[?blée] [?a] Erfort pour traicter une ligue deffensive, craignans d'estre assaillis par […] le
comte […] plus que nul des autres. Il [dict] que la royne d'Angleterre y a son ambassadeur, il est apres pour entandre ce qu'on y deliberera, dont il m'a promis m'adviser. Il m'a aussi dict qu'encores que quelqu'ung l'ayt adverty qu'il se
faisoit [?levée] de gens pour aller en France, qu'il n'y [a] aulcun fondement; et qu'il a donné si bon ordre que s'il en est quelque chose, qu'il en sera adverty.
Translation

1569-10-20_en.md

<!-- English translation of read/1569-10-20_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026). Same holes. -->

# 20 Oct 1569, Scipion de Fiesque to the King, Vienna

[v159r] ⟨… to this war.⟩ And since he has heard that the Protestants and several others of their
religion are holding an assembly [?at] Erfurt to negotiate a defensive league, fearing to be attacked by […] Count […]
more than by any of the others. He [says] that the Queen of England has her ambassador there, who is about to learn what is decided there, of which he has promised to inform me. He also told me that, although someone had informed him that a levy
of men was being made to go to France, there is no foundation for it; and that he has given such good orders that if there is anything he will be informed of it.

29 Oct 1569, to the King (f158)

Scipion de Fiesque, French envoy at the court of Emperor Maximilian II, to the King, Vienna, 29 Oct 1569, during the negotiation of Charles IX's marriage with Elisabeth of Austria. Contents (conjectural, from the contributor's page list): After the victory (taken to be Moncontour), the Emperor offers to mediate.

<!-- Reading of the ciphered passage, built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (REREAD_BRIEF, batch b02): the key decode
(contributed/data/decoded_f158.txt; Tomokiyo's key as applied by Harumi-Matsuba) divided into words and read sentence by
sentence, with the known sign confusions resolved (y-shape s/p, n/y in "ronaume" = royaume, a/o = e) and 16 = vostre.
Checked against the contributor's line readings in contributed/docs/READING.en.md (f. 158r), which agree where they
read; they were not used as a source. No contemporary decipherment of this letter is known; no image check. Original

# 29 Oct 1569, Scipion de Fiesque to the King, Vienna (BnF fr. 15919, f. 158r, view 163 right)

[f158r] ⟨…⟩ avec la [?copie] de la responce qu'avez faicte a [∞ l'Empereur], auquel j'ay dict comme vous estiez prest
a partir pour aller trouver vostre camp, pour poursuivre le fruict de la victoire que Dieu vous a donnée, [?ou] seront tous les principaulx seigneurs et cappitaines de vostre royaume, avec lesquelz prandre quelque bonne resolution; et mesmes sur
l'[?offre] qu'il vous a faict de se vouloir employer a traicter quelque bonne pacification sur les troubles qui sont; et
[que] m'avez commandé de le remercier bien fort de la demonstration qu'il faict d'avoir tousjours en singuliere
recommandation le bien, prouffit et utilité de vostre royaume, et que [vous] respondriez de pareille affection en tout ce
qui l'en touchera. Lequel m'a dict qu'il ne s'est meu a mettre en avant ce party que pour une sincere affection et grand
desir qu'il a de [vous] veoir delivré de ceste guerre intestine, et mesmement a ceste heure qu'il n'aura [que] ceste
estroicte liaison de vostre mariage avec sa fille, s'offrant de faire tout ce que sera en son pouvoir; estant en la
mesme opinion qu'il a tousjours esté, que en ce que pourriez remedier avec douceur, que n'y usiez de rigueur, estant
[?de] nature vo[…] tousjours [?si bien] aymé de ces subjectz.
Translation

1569-10-29_en.md

<!-- English translation of read/1569-10-29_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026). Same holes. -->

# 29 Oct 1569, Scipion de Fiesque to the King, Vienna

[f158r] ⟨…⟩ with the [?copy] of the answer you made to [the Emperor], whom I told how you were ready to leave to join
your camp, to pursue the fruit of the victory that God has given you, [?where] all the principal lords [will be] and captains of your
kingdom, with whom to take some good decision; and also about the [?offer] he made you to be willing to employ himself to
negotiate some good pacification of the present troubles; and [that] you commanded me to thank him very warmly for the
demonstration he gives of always holding in singular regard the good, profit and advantage of your kingdom, and that
[you] would answer with like affection in everything that concerns him. He told me that he was moved to put forward this
course only by a sincere affection and great desire to see [you] delivered from this civil war, and especially now that
he will have this close bond of your marriage with his daughter, offering to do everything in his power; being of the
same opinion he has always been, that where you could remedy things with gentleness you should not use rigour, being
[?by] nature [your …] always [?so well] loved by these subjects.

4 Nov 1569, to the Queen Mother (v168r)

Scipion de Fiesque, French envoy at the court of Emperor Maximilian II, to the Queen Mother, Vienna, 4 Nov 1569, during the negotiation of Charles IX's marriage with Elisabeth of Austria. Contents (conjectural, from the contributor's page list): The courier; the answer; ceremonies of the marriage.

# 4 Nov 1569, Scipion de Fiesque to the Queen Mother, Vienna (BnF fr. 15919, view 168 right)

[v168r] J'ay retenu le courrier pour vous mander par luy la responce que me fera [ϖ] sur ce que je luy ay
demandé touchant ce que m'escripvez des ceremonies qu'il entend que se facent au mariage, affin que [?prenies] resolution
d'y envoyer tel personnaige qu'il appartiendra. Lequel m'a dict qu'il me randra bientost responce et resolution,
laquelle ayant, je ne faillray vous envoyer incontinant par ledict courrier.

1569-11-04_en.md

<!-- English translation of read/1569-11-04_reading.md (Claude Opus 5.5, 22 Oct 2026). Same holes. -->

# 4 Nov 1569, Scipion de Fiesque to the Queen Mother, Vienna

[v168r] I have kept back the courier in order to send you by him the answer that [ϖ] (the Emperor) will give me on what I
asked him concerning what you write to me about the ceremonies he intends to be performed at the marriage, so that you
[?may] decide to send such a person as is fitting. He told me that he will soon give me an answer and decision, and
having it I shall not fail to send it to you at once by the said courier.
